## QAA-156 · L3 · hard · Ages — present, past and future · officer

The ratio of A's age five years ago to B's age five years hence is 1 : 2, and A's age five years hence equals B's age five years ago. A's present age is:

- **A.** 35 years  _(error: B's age reported)_
- **B.** 30 years  _(error: A's age five years hence)_
- **C.** 25 years ✅
- **D.** 20 years  _(error: A's age five years ago)_

**Working**

1. (A − 5)/(B + 5) = 1/2 and A + 5 = B − 5
2. B = A + 10 ⇒ 2A − 10 = A + 15 ⇒ A = 25

**Formula:** Translate each clause to an equation  
**Trap:** Keep 'ago' and 'hence' on the right person.

**Reviewer:** ☐ key ☐ stem ☐ distractors ☐ level ☐ fact — notes:

## QAA-157 · L3 · hard · Ages — present, past and future · officer

A father's age is 4 years more than three times his son's. After 8 years, the father's age will be 10 years more than twice the son's. The father's present age is:

- **A.** 54 years  _(error: father's age after 8 years)_
- **B.** 46 years ✅
- **C.** 14 years  _(error: son's age reported)_
- **D.** 16 years  _(error: the extra 10 years left out)_

**Working**

1. F = 3S + 4
2. 3S + 12 = 2S + 26 ⇒ S = 14
3. F = 46

**Formula:** Substitute F in the future equation  
**Trap:** Add 8 to both before comparing.

## QAA-158 · L3 · hard · Ages — present, past and future · officer

- **B.** 18 years  _(error: ageing counted as 5 years in total)_
- **C.** 23 years  _(error: ageing of the four ignored)_
- **D.** 8 years  _(error: ageing counted for three members)_

**Working**

1. Sum five years ago = 120; now for the four = 140
2. Family sum now = 5 × 28.6 = 143
3. Baby = 3 years

**Formula:** Each of the four adds 5 years

## QAA-159 · L3 · hard · Ages — present, past and future · officer

The ages of A and B are in the ratio 5 : 3. When A was as old as B is now, the sum of their ages was 28 years. A's present age is:

- **A.** 21 years  _(error: B's age reported)_
- **B.** 28 years  _(error: the sum itself)_
- **C.** 35 years ✅
- **D.** 17.5 years  _(error: 28 taken as present sum)_

**Working**

1. A = 5x, B = 3x; 2x years ago A was 3x, B was x
2. 3x + x = 28 ⇒ x = 7
3. A = 35

**Formula:** Go back by the age gap

## QAA-160 · L3 · hard · Ages — present, past and future · officer

The product of the present ages of two brothers is 240. Four years ago the product was 128. The elder brother's present age is:

- **A.** 16 years  _(error: half the sum taken)_
- **B.** 24 years  _(error: elder's age four years hence)_
- **C.** 20 years ✅
- **D.** 12 years  _(error: younger brother's age)_

**Working**

1. (a − 4)(b − 4) = ab − 4(a + b) + 16
2. 128 = 240 − 4S + 16 ⇒ S = 32
3. Ages 12 and 20

**Formula:** Expand the product  
**Trap:** Sum from the product condition, then factor.

## QAA-161 · L3 · hard · Ages — present, past and future · officer

Rita is 5 years younger than Sunil. Seven years ago Rita's age was 3/4 of Sunil's. The ratio of their ages 3 years from now will be:

- **A.** 3:4  _(error: past ratio assumed)_
- **B.** 25:27  _(error: 3 years added to Rita only)_
- **C.** 22:27  _(error: present ratio)_
- **D.** 5:6 ✅

**Working**

1. R = S − 5; 4(S − 12) = 3(S − 7) ⇒ S = 27
2. R = 22
3. In 3 years: 25 : 30 = 5 : 6

**Formula:** Solve for present ages

2. Average = 130/5 = 26

## QAA-163 · L1 · easy · Averages — simple and weighted · foundation

The average mark of 40 students is 62. When the teacher's mark of 85 is included, the new average is:

## QAA-164 · L2 · medium · Averages — simple and weighted · foundation

The average of six numbers is 30. The average of the first four is 26, and the fifth number is 8 more than the sixth. The fifth number is:

## QAA-167 · L3 · hard · Averages — simple and weighted · officer

## QAA-168 · L3 · hard · Averages — simple and weighted · officer

## QAA-170 · L3 · hard · Averages — simple and weighted · officer

## QAA-174 · L3 · hard · Averages — simple and weighted · officer

The average of 7 consecutive integers is n. If the next three integers are included, the new average is:

- **A.** n + 1  _(error: one step added)_
- **B.** n + 1.5 ✅
- **C.** n + 3  _(error: average of the three new numbers minus n)_
- **D.** n + 5  _(error: middle of the new three)_

**Working**

1. Numbers n − 3 … n + 3; new ones n + 4, n + 5, n + 6
2. Sum = 7n + 3n + 15 = 10n + 15
3. Average = n + 1.5

**Formula:** Mean of consecutive integers = middle  
**Trap:** New list runs n − 3 to n + 6; middle is n + 1.5.

**Reviewer:** ☐ key ☐ stem ☐ distractors ☐ level ☐ fact — notes:

## QAA-175 · L3 · hard · Averages — simple and weighted · officer

The average age of 30 students is 14.5 years. Five students averaging 16 years leave and eight averaging 13 years join. The new average age is:

- **A.** 14.5  _(error: assumes average unchanged)_
- **B.** 15.3  _(error: divided by 30)_
- **C.** 14.2  _(error: joiners ignored)_
- **D.** 13.91 ✅

**Working**

1. Total = 435 − 80 + 104 = 459
2. 459/33 = 13.91

**Formula:** Adjust total and count  
**Trap:** Count becomes 33.

**Reviewer:** ☐ key ☐ stem ☐ distractors ☐ level ☐ fact — notes:

## QAA-176 · L1 · easy · Median and mode · foundation

The median of 7, 3, 9, 12, 5, 8, 10 is:

- **A.** 8 ✅
- **B.** 9  _(error: off-by-one position)_
- **C.** 7.71  _(error: mean reported)_
- **D.** 12  _(error: middle of unsorted list)_

**Working**

1. Sorted: 3, 5, 7, 8, 9, 10, 12
2. 4th value = 8

**Formula:** Median = middle of sorted data  
**Trap:** Sort first.

- **A.** 4 ✅
- **B.** 5.56  _(error: mean reported)_
- **C.** 6  _(error: second most frequent)_
- **D.** 9  _(error: largest value)_

**Working**

1. 4 occurs 4 times; 6 occurs 3 times
2. Mode = 4

**Formula:** Mode = most frequent value

## QAA-178 · L2 · medium · Median and mode · foundation

The median of 15, 22, 9, 31, 18, 27, 12, 20, 25, 14, 29, 17 is:

- **A.** 19.92  _(error: mean reported)_
- **B.** 19 ✅
- **C.** 18  _(error: 6th value only)_
- **D.** 20  _(error: 7th value only)_

**Working**

1. Sorted: 9, 12, 14, 15, 17, 18, 20, 22, 25, 27, 29, 31
2. Median = (18 + 20)/2 = 19

**Formula:** Even n: average the two middle values  
**Trap:** Twelve values: 6th and 7th.

**Reviewer:** ☐ key ☐ stem ☐ distractors ☐ level ☐ fact — notes:

## QAA-179 · L2 · medium · Median and mode · foundation

In a moderately skewed distribution the median is 24 and the mean is 26. Using the empirical relation, the mode is approximately:

- **A.** 20 ✅
- **B.** 30  _(error: 3 × mean − 2 × median)_
- **C.** 28  _(error: mean + (mean − median))_
- **D.** 22  _(error: mode = 2 × median − mean)_

**Working**

1. Mode = 3 Median − 2 Mean
2. = 72 − 52 = 20

**Formula:** Mode ≈ 3 Median − 2 Mean  
**Trap:** Coefficients 3 and 2 go on median and mean.

**Reviewer:** ☐ key ☐ stem ☐ distractors ☐ level ☐ fact — notes:

## QAA-180 · L2 · medium · Median and mode · foundation

The data 5, 8, x, x + 2, 15, 18 are in ascending order and their median is 11. The value of x is:

- **A.** 12  _(error: x + 1 taken as 13)_
- **B.** 11  _(error: median taken as x)_
- **C.** 10 ✅
- **D.** 9  _(error: median taken as x + 2)_

**Working**

1. Median = (x + x + 2)/2 = x + 1
2. x + 1 = 11 ⇒ x = 10

**Formula:** Even n: average of 3rd and 4th  
**Trap:** Median is x + 1, not x.

**Reviewer:** ☐ key ☐ stem ☐ distractors ☐ level ☐ fact — notes:

## QAA-181 · L3 · hard · Median and mode · officer

The median of 25 observations is 40. If each of the largest 5 is increased by 10 and each of the smallest 3 is decreased by 5, the new median is:

- **A.** 45  _(error: largest-value change applied)_
- **B.** 40 ✅
- **C.** 41.4  _(error: net shift in total spread over 25 (mean logic))_
- **D.** 35  _(error: smallest-value change applied)_

**Working**

1. Median is the 13th value
2. Changes affect only positions 1–3 and 21–25
3. 13th value unchanged ⇒ 40

**Formula:** Median depends on position  
**Trap:** Extreme-value changes do not move the median here.

**Reviewer:** ☐ key ☐ stem ☐ distractors ☐ level ☐ fact — notes:

## QAA-182 · L3 · hard · Median and mode · officer

For the data 6, 9, 14, 9, x, 21, 14, 9, 14 the mode is 14 and it is the only mode. The median of the data is:

- **A.** 12.22  _(error: mean reported)_
- **B.** 14 ✅
- **C.** 9  _(error: mode taken as 9)_
- **D.** 11.5  _(error: average of the two frequent values)_

**Working**

1. Without x, 9 and 14 each occur 3 times
2. A unique mode 14 needs x = 14
3. Sorted: 6, 9, 9, 9, 14, 14, 14, 14, 21 ⇒ median 14

**Formula:** Median = 5th of 9 sorted values  
**Trap:** Find x from the mode condition first.

**Reviewer:** ☐ key ☐ stem ☐ distractors ☐ level ☐ fact — notes:

## QAA-183 · L3 · hard · Median and mode · officer

Marks (frequency): 10 (4), 20 (7), 30 (12), 40 (9), 50 (8). The mean exceeds the mode by:

- **A.** −2.5  _(error: subtraction reversed)_
- **B.** 2.5 ✅
- **C.** 0  _(error: mean assumed equal to mode)_
- **D.** −7.5  _(error: mode taken as 40)_

**Working**

1. Mean = 1,300/40 = 32.5
2. Mode = 30 (highest frequency 12)
3. Difference = 2.5

**Formula:** Mean = Σfx/Σf  
**Trap:** Mode is the value, not the frequency.

**Reviewer:** ☐ key ☐ stem ☐ distractors ☐ level ☐ fact — notes:

## QAA-184 · L3 · hard · Median and mode · officer

Nine numbers have mean 20 and median 18. Each number is multiplied by 3 and then 5 is subtracted. The new mean and median are:

- **A.** 15 and 13  _(error: only the subtraction applied)_
- **B.** 60 and 54  _(error: subtraction of 5 missed)_
- **C.** 55 and 49 ✅
- **D.** 55 and 54  _(error: median not reduced by 5)_

**Working**

1. Mean: 3 × 20 − 5 = 55
2. Median: 3 × 18 − 5 = 49

**Formula:** Linear transforms act on mean and median alike  
**Trap:** Apply both steps to both measures.

**Reviewer:** ☐ key ☐ stem ☐ distractors ☐ level ☐ fact — notes:

## QAA-185 · L3 · hard · Median and mode · officer

Consider:

I. The median is not affected by changing the extreme values.
II. A data set can have more than one mode.
III. The mean always lies between the median and the mode.

Which are correct?

- **A.** I, II and III  _(error: empirical relation treated as a law)_
- **B.** II and III only  _(error: III accepted as a rule)_
- **C.** I only  _(error: II wrongly rejected)_
- **D.** I and II only ✅

**Working**

1. I: true as long as the order of middle values is unchanged
2. II: true (bimodal data)
3. III: false — data 0, 6, 6, 7, 8 has mode 6, median 6, mean 5.4

**Formula:** Mode ≈ 3 Median − 2 Mean is only approximate  
**Trap:** III fails for many data sets.

**Reviewer:** ☐ key ☐ stem ☐ distractors ☐ level ☐ fact — notes:

## QAA-186 · L3 · hard · Median and mode · officer

Five positive integers have mean 12, median 11 and a unique mode of 8. The largest possible value of the greatest integer is:

- **A.** 17  _(error: minimised instead of maximised)_
- **B.** 21 ✅
- **C.** 33  _(error: fourth number ignored)_
- **D.** 22  _(error: fourth number allowed to be 11, creating a tie)_

**Working**

1. Order: 8, 8, 11, d, e with sum 60
2. d + e = 33; d ≥ 12 (d = 11 would tie with 8 as mode)
3. Max e = 33 − 12 = 21

**Formula:** Fix the known values; push the rest to the extreme  
**Trap:** A repeated 11 would break the unique mode.

**Reviewer:** ☐ key ☐ stem ☐ distractors ☐ level ☐ fact — notes:

## QAA-187 · L2 · medium · Median and mode · officer

The median of the first 20 even natural numbers is:

- **A.** 20.5  _(error: average of 1…40 range misread)_
- **B.** 21 ✅
- **C.** 22  _(error: 11th value only)_
- **D.** 20  _(error: 10th value only)_

**Working**

1. Numbers 2, 4, …, 40
2. Median = (20 + 22)/2 = 21

**Formula:** Even n: average two middle

- **A.** 30 L  _(error: milk reported)_

- **C.** 12 L  _(error: water taken as 30%)_
- **D.** 13.33 L  _(error: ratio 3 : 1 read as 1/3)_

**Working**

1. Water = 40 × 1/4 = 10 L

## QAA-189 · L2 · medium · Mixtures and alligation · foundation

- **A.** 2:1 ✅
- **B.** 3:1  _(error: difference 12 split as 9 : 3)_
- **C.** 4:3  _(error: ratio of prices 44 : 32 reduced)_
- **D.** 1:2  _(error: alligation sides swapped)_

**Working**

1. Cheap : Dear = (44 − 36) : (36 − 32) = 8 : 4 = 2 : 1

**Formula:** Alligation

1. Milk 40, water 20

## QAA-191 · L2 · medium · Mixtures and alligation · foundation

In what ratio should a 30% acid solution be mixed with a 50% acid solution to get a 36% solution?

- **A.** 1:1  _(error: simple average assumed)_
- **B.** 3:2  _(error: rough split)_
- **C.** 3:7  _(error: alligation sides swapped)_
- **D.** 7:3 ✅

**Working**

1. 30% : 50% = (50 − 36) : (36 − 30) = 14 : 6 = 7 : 3

**Formula:** Alligation  
**Trap:** 36 is nearer 30, so more of the 30% solution.

## QAA-192 · L1 · easy · Mixtures and alligation · foundation

25 litres of a 20% salt solution is diluted with water to make a 10% solution. The water added is:

- **A.** 50 L  _(error: final volume reported)_
- **B.** 25 L ✅
- **C.** 20 L  _(error: rate read as litres)_
- **D.** 12.5 L  _(error: half the original volume)_

**Working**

1. Salt = 5 L
2. Final volume = 5/0.10 = 50 L
3. Water added = 25 L

**Formula:** Solute fixed: C₁V₁ = C₂V₂  
**Trap:** Subtract the original volume.

## QAA-193 · L3 · hard · Mixtures and alligation · officer

From 80 L of pure milk, 8 L is removed and replaced with water. This is done three times in all. The milk left is:

- **A.** 58.32 L ✅
- **B.** 64.8 L  _(error: only two operations)_
- **C.** 56 L  _(error: 8 L of milk removed each time)_
- **D.** 57.6 L  _(error: rough 28% loss)_

**Working**

1. Fraction left each time = 72/80 = 0.9
2. Milk = 80 × 0.9³ = 58.32 L

**Formula:** Final = Initial × (1 − x/V)^n  
**Trap:** Later removals take out less milk.

**Reviewer:** ☐ key ☐ stem ☐ distractors ☐ level ☐ fact — notes:

## QAA-194 · L3 · hard · Mixtures and alligation · officer

Vessel A has milk and water in the ratio 5 : 3; vessel B in the ratio 3 : 1. In what ratio should A and B be mixed to get milk and water in the ratio 7 : 3?

- **A.** 5:3  _(error: A's own ratio reported)_
- **B.** 2:3 ✅
- **C.** 3:2  _(error: alligation sides swapped)_
- **D.** 1:1  _(error: equal mixing assumed)_

**Working**

1. Milk fractions: A = 5/8, B = 3/4, target = 7/10
2. A : B = (3/4 − 7/10) : (7/10 − 5/8) = 1/20 : 3/40 = 2 : 3

**Formula:** Alligate on milk fractions  
**Trap:** Convert ratios to fractions of the whole.

**Reviewer:** ☐ key ☐ stem ☐ distractors ☐ level ☐ fact — notes:

## QAA-195 · L3 · hard · Mixtures and alligation · officer

A 60-litre can holds milk and water in the ratio 7 : 5. How much milk must be added to make the ratio 9 : 5?

- **A.** 10 L ✅

1. Milk 35, water 25
2. Water fixed: milk must be 9/5 × 25 = 45

2. Final volume = 32/0.64 = 50 L

## QAA-197 · L3 · hard · Mixtures and alligation · officer

- **D.** ₹198  _(error: three equal parts assumed)_

1. 126 + 135 + 2x = 4 × 153 = 612
2. 2x = 351 ⇒ x = 175.5

## QAA-198 · L3 · hard · Mixtures and alligation · officer

A cask holds 50 L of wine. 10 L is drawn off and replaced with water; then 10 L of the mixture is drawn off and replaced with water. The ratio of wine to water now is:

- **A.** 16:9 ✅
- **B.** 9:16  _(error: ratio inverted)_
- **C.** 3:2  _(error: 10 L of pure wine removed each time)_
- **D.** 4:1  _(error: only one replacement)_

**Working**

1. Wine = 50 × (4/5)² = 32 L
2. Water = 18 L ⇒ 16 : 9

**Formula:** Final = V(1 − x/V)^n  
**Trap:** Second draw removes mixture, not pure wine.

## QAA-199 · L3 · hard · Mixtures and alligation · officer

A milkman mixes water with milk costing ₹50/L and sells the mixture at ₹50/L, gaining 25%. The ratio of milk to water in the mixture is:

- **A.** 1:4  _(error: ratio inverted)_
- **B.** 3:1  _(error: 25% gain taken on SP)_
- **C.** 4:1 ✅
- **D.** 5:1  _(error: 20% gain scenario)_

**Working**

1. Cost of 1 L mixture = 50/1.25 = ₹40
2. Milk fraction = 40/50 = 4/5
3. Milk : water = 4 : 1

**Formula:** Gain comes from free water  
**Trap:** Profit is on cost.

## QAA-201 · L2 · medium · Partnership and profit sharing · foundation

**Working**

1. A: 3,60,000; B: 3,60,000 capital-months
2. Equal ⇒ B = 18,000

## QAA-203 · L2 · medium · Partnership and profit sharing · foundation

## QAA-205 · L3 · hard · Partnership and profit sharing · officer

A starts with ₹60,000 and withdraws ₹20,000 after 3 months. B starts with ₹50,000 and adds ₹10,000 after 6 months. Out of the year's profit of ₹1,02,000, A's share is:

## QAA-208 · L3 · hard · Partnership and profit sharing · officer

A invests ₹24,000 at the start; B joins some months later with ₹36,000. At year end profit is shared equally. B joined after:

## QAA-209 · L3 · hard · Partnership and profit sharing · officer

- **C.** ₹1,08,571.43  _(error: salary added to 2/5 of full profit)_

## QAA-210 · L3 · hard · Partnership and profit sharing · officer

In a firm A gets 1/3 of the profit, B gets 1/4 and C the rest. C's share is ₹10,000 more than B's. The total profit is:

- **A.** ₹60,000 ✅
- **B.** ₹40,000  _(error: gap taken as 1/4)_
- **C.** ₹24,000  _(error: C's share taken as ₹10,000)_
- **D.** ₹1,20,000  _(error: gap taken as C − A)_

**Working**

1. C = 1 − 1/3 − 1/4 = 5/12
2. C − B = 5/12 − 3/12 = 1/6
3. P = 10,000 × 6 = 60,000

**Formula:** Fractions of the whole  
**Trap:** Compare C with B.

**Reviewer:** ☐ key ☐ stem ☐ distractors ☐ level ☐ fact — notes:

## QAA-211 · L3 · hard · Partnership and profit sharing · officer

A and B invest in the ratio 3 : 5. After 4 months A withdraws half his capital; after 8 months B withdraws one-fifth of his. The ratio of their annual profits is:

- **A.** 2:5  _(error: B's withdrawal ignored)_
- **B.** 9:14  _(error: A's withdrawal ignored)_
- **C.** 3:7 ✅
- **D.** 3:5  _(error: withdrawals ignored)_

**Working**

1. A: 3 × 4 + 1.5 × 8 = 24
2. B: 5 × 8 + 4 × 4 = 56
3. 24 : 56 = 3 : 7

**Formula:** Σ capital × months  
**Trap:** Each partner changes at a different time.

**Reviewer:** ☐ key ☐ stem ☐ distractors ☐ level ☐ fact — notes:

1. Larger = 7,200 × 7/12 = 4,200

**Formula:** Share = total × term/sum

- **D.** ₹750 ✅

**Working**

1. A : B : C = 8 : 12 : 15
2. C = 1,750 × 15/35 = 750

## QAA-217 · L3 · hard · Proportional division of quantities · officer

**Working**

1. Remainders total 6,200 − 200 = 6,000
2. C's remainder = 6,000 × 5/12 = 2,500
3. C = 2,580

**Formula:** Remove the deductions first

## QAA-218 · L3 · hard · Proportional division of quantities · officer

A bag holds ₹1, 50-paise and 25-paise coins in the ratio 2 : 3 : 5 by number, worth ₹95 in all. The number of 25-paise coins is:

3. 25-paise coins = 5 × 20 = 100

**Formula:** Value = count × denomination

- **D.** ₹7,700 ✅

2. Second = 18,700 × 7/17 = 7,700

## QAA-221 · L3 · hard · Proportional division of quantities · officer

An amount is divided among A, B and C. A gets 1/3 of what B and C together get, and B gets 2/3 of what A and C together get. If C gets ₹4,200, the total is:

- **A.** ₹50,400  _(error: B taken as 2/3 of total)_
- **B.** ₹12,000 ✅
- **C.** ₹15,750  _(error: A taken as 1/3 of total)_
- **D.** ₹8,400  _(error: fractions added to 1 and doubled)_

**Working**

1. A = (T − A)/3 ⇒ A = T/4
2. B = 2(T − B)/3 ⇒ B = 2T/5
3. C = T − T/4 − 2T/5 = 7T/20 = 4,200 ⇒ T = 12,000

**Formula:** Convert 'of the others' into 'of the total'  
**Trap:** A = k(T − A) ⇒ A = kT/(1 + k).

**Reviewer:** ☐ key ☐ stem ☐ distractors ☐ level ☐ fact — notes:

## QAA-222 · L3 · hard · Proportional division of quantities · officer

Boys and girls in a class are in the ratio 7 : 5. After 12 more girls join, the ratio becomes 7 : 6. The total strength now is:

- **A.** 144  _(error: strength before)_
- **B.** 168  _(error: new girls counted twice)_
- **C.** 156 ✅
- **D.** 132  _(error: girls subtracted)_

**Working**

1. 7x/(5x + 12) = 7/6 ⇒ 42x = 35x + 84 ⇒ x = 12
2. Now 84 + 72 = 156

**Formula:** Boys fixed  
**Trap:** Add the 12 new girls.

## QAA-225 · L3 · hard · Proportional division of quantities · officer

₹39,000 is divided among A, B and C so that A gets ₹3,000 more than B and B gets ₹4,500 more than C. A's share is:

- **A.** ₹9,000  _(error: C's share reported)_
- **B.** ₹13,000  _(error: equal split)_
- **C.** ₹16,500 ✅

1. A = C + 7,500, B = C + 4,500
2. 3C + 12,000 = 39,000 ⇒ C = 9,000

## QAA-230 · L2 · medium · Simple and compound ratio · foundation

## QAA-239 · L3 · hard · Simple and compound ratio · officer

The third proportional to (x² − y²) and (x − y) is:

- **A.** (x − y)²  _(error: denominator dropped)_
- **B.** x + y  _(error: cancelled wrongly)_
- **C.** (x + y)/(x − y)  _(error: inverted)_
- **D.** (x − y)/(x + y) ✅

**Working**

1. a : b = b : c ⇒ c = b²/a
2. c = (x − y)²/[(x − y)(x + y)] = (x − y)/(x + y)

## QAA-240 · L1 · easy · Average speed and journey segments · foundation

A car goes from P to Q at 60 km/h and returns at 40 km/h. Its average speed for the round trip is:

- **A.** 24 km/h  _(error: half the harmonic mean)_
- **B.** 100 km/h  _(error: speeds added)_
- **C.** 50 km/h  _(error: arithmetic mean of speeds)_
- **D.** 48 km/h ✅

**Working**

1. Average = 2 × 60 × 40/(60 + 40) = 48

**Formula:** Equal distances ⇒ harmonic mean  
**Trap:** Time is longer on the slow leg.

## QAA-241 · L1 · easy · Average speed and journey segments · foundation

- **A.** 52.5 km/h  _(error: average of the two speeds)_

- **D.** 60 km/h  _(error: first-leg speed)_

1. Total 300 km in 6 h
2. Average = 50 km/h

**Formula:** Average speed = total distance ÷ total time

## QAA-242 · L2 · medium · Average speed and journey segments · foundation

A cyclist covers equal thirds of a route at 30, 40 and 60 km/h. The average speed is:

- **A.** 40 km/h ✅
- **B.** 45 km/h  _(error: middle and top speed averaged)_
- **C.** 34.29 km/h  _(error: harmonic mean of the two slower speeds only)_
- **D.** 43 1/3 km/h  _(error: arithmetic mean)_

**Working**

1. Time per unit third: 1/30 + 1/40 + 1/60 = 9/120
2. Average = 3 ÷ 9/120 = 40

**Formula:** Equal distances: n ÷ Σ(1/vᵢ)  
**Trap:** Use the harmonic mean.

## QAA-243 · L2 · medium · Average speed and journey segments · foundation

1. Equal times ⇒ distances 50t and 70t
2. Average = 120t/2t = 60

**Formula:** Equal times ⇒ arithmetic mean

## QAA-244 · L2 · medium · Average speed and journey segments · foundation

## QAA-245 · L3 · hard · Average speed and journey segments · officer

- **A.** 71 1/9 km/h ✅

1. Times 2.5 h and 2 h
2. Average = 320/4.5 = 71 1/9

## QAA-246 · L3 · hard · Average speed and journey segments · officer

A trekker climbs a hill at 12 km/h and comes down the same path at 18 km/h, taking 5 hours in all. The one-way distance is:

- **A.** 30 km  _(error: slower speed × 2.5 h)_
- **B.** 37.5 km  _(error: average of speeds used)_
- **C.** 36 km ✅
- **D.** 72 km  _(error: round-trip distance)_

**Working**

1. d/12 + d/18 = 5 ⇒ 5d/36 = 5
2. d = 36 km

**Formula:** Σ(d/vᵢ) = total time  
**Trap:** Arithmetic mean overstates the pace.

**Reviewer:** ☐ key ☐ stem ☐ distractors ☐ level ☐ fact — notes:

## QAA-247 · L3 · hard · Average speed and journey segments · officer

A car travels at 45 km/h for the first 30 minutes. At what speed must it travel for the next 1.5 hours to average 60 km/h over the 2 hours?

- **A.** 80 km/h  _(error: first 22.5 km not subtracted)_
- **B.** 97.5 km/h  _(error: remaining distance read as speed)_
- **C.** 65 km/h ✅
- **D.** 75 km/h  _(error: speeds averaged: (45 + x)/2 = 60)_

**Working**

1. Required total = 120 km
2. Covered = 22.5 km
3. Speed = 97.5/1.5 = 65

**Formula:** Remaining distance ÷ remaining time  
**Trap:** Weights are the times.

**Reviewer:** ☐ key ☐ stem ☐ distractors ☐ level ☐ fact — notes:

## QAA-248 · L3 · hard · Average speed and journey segments · officer

A cyclist rides 12 km at 8 km/h, rests for 30 minutes, then rides 18 km at 12 km/h. His average speed for the whole journey, including the rest, is:

- **A.** 6.67 km/h  _(error: mean of 8, 0 and 12)_
- **B.** 8.57 km/h ✅
- **C.** 9.09 km/h  _(error: 30 min taken as 0.3 h)_
- **D.** 10 km/h  _(error: rest excluded)_

**Working**

1. Riding times 1.5 h + 1.5 h; rest 0.5 h
2. Average = 30/3.5 = 8.57

**Formula:** Include stoppage time  
**Trap:** Rest adds time, not distance.

**Reviewer:** ☐ key ☐ stem ☐ distractors ☐ level ☐ fact — notes:

## QAA-249 · L3 · hard · Average speed and journey segments · officer

Running at 3/4 of its usual speed, a train reaches 20 minutes late. Its usual journey time is:

- **A.** 1 h 20 min  _(error: late-running time reported)_
- **B.** 45 min  _(error: usual time × 3/4)_
- **C.** 1 h ✅
- **D.** 15 min  _(error: 20 × 3/4)_

**Working**

1. Time becomes 4/3 of usual
2. Extra = 1/3 of usual = 20 min ⇒ usual = 60 min

**Formula:** Time ∝ 1/speed  
**Trap:** Extra time is 1/3 of usual time.

**Reviewer:** ☐ key ☐ stem ☐ distractors ☐ level ☐ fact — notes:

## QAA-250 · L3 · hard · Average speed and journey segments · officer

Walking at 5 km/h a man misses his train by 7 minutes; walking at 6 km/h he reaches 5 minutes early. The distance to the station is:

- **A.** 6 km ✅
- **B.** 12 km  _(error: minutes read as km)_
- **C.** 3.6 km  _(error: 12 min taken as 0.12 h)_
- **D.** 1 km  _(error: time gap taken as 2 min (7 − 5))_

**Working**

1. d/5 − d/6 = 12/60
2. d/30 = 1/5 ⇒ d = 6 km

**Formula:** Time gap = late + early  
**Trap:** Minutes late and early add up.

## QAA-251 · L3 · hard · Average speed and journey segments · officer

A courier covers four equal legs of a route at 20, 30, 60 and 60 km/h. The average speed for the route is:

- **A.** 34.29 km/h ✅
- **B.** 30 km/h  _(error: repeated 60 km/h leg counted once)_
- **C.** 42.5 km/h  _(error: arithmetic mean)_
- **D.** 40 km/h  _(error: rough midpoint)_

**Working**

1. Time per unit leg: 1/20 + 1/30 + 1/60 + 1/60 = 7/60
2. Average = 4 ÷ 7/60 = 34.29

**Formula:** Equal distances: n ÷ Σ(1/v)  
**Trap:** Every leg counts.

## QAA-252 · L3 · hard · Average speed and journey segments · officer

An athlete's speeds over the two halves of a run are in the ratio 3 : 4, and his average speed for the whole run is 48 km/h. His speed in the first half is:

- **A.** 42 km/h ✅

- **C.** 56 km/h  _(error: second-half speed)_
- **D.** 41.14 km/h  _(error: arithmetic-mean relation used)_

**Working**

1. Speeds 3k, 4k; harmonic mean = 24k/7 = 48
2. k = 14 ⇒ first half 42 km/h

**Formula:** Equal halves by distance ⇒ harmonic mean

## QAA-253 · L1 · easy · Relative speed and meeting problems · foundation

**Working**

1. Closing speed = 12 km/h
2. Time = 60/12 = 5 h

**Formula:** Opposite directions: add speeds

## QAA-254 · L1 · easy · Relative speed and meeting problems · foundation

**Working**

1. Relative speed = 2 km/h = 2,000/60 m/min
2. Time = 300 ÷ (100/3) = 9 min

## QAA-256 · L2 · medium · Relative speed and meeting problems · foundation

- **C.** 150 km  _(error: midpoint assumed)_

1. Time = 300/100 = 3 h
2. A covers 40 × 3 = 120 km

**Formula:** Distances ∝ speeds

## QAA-257 · L2 · medium · Relative speed and meeting problems · foundation

1. Relative speed = 3 km/h
2. Time = 9/3 = 3 h

## QAA-258 · L3 · hard · Relative speed and meeting problems · officer

Two trains start at the same time from A and B towards each other. After meeting, they take 9 h and 16 h to reach B and A respectively. The ratio of their speeds (train from A : train from B) is:

- **A.** 4:3 ✅
- **B.** 9:16  _(error: times used directly)_
- **C.** 16:9  _(error: times inverted without roots)_
- **D.** 3:4  _(error: square roots inverted)_

**Working**

1. S₁ : S₂ = √t₂ : √t₁
2. = √16 : √9 = 4 : 3

**Formula:** Speed ratio = √(t₂/t₁)  
**Trap:** Take square roots.

## QAA-259 · L3 · hard · Relative speed and meeting problems · officer

A leaves X at 8:00 am at 40 km/h. B leaves X at 10:00 am on the same road at 60 km/h. B overtakes A at:

- **A.** 2:00 pm ✅
- **B.** 1:20 pm  _(error: 80 km ÷ 60 counted from noon)_
- **C.** 10:48 am  _(error: speeds added)_
- **D.** 12:00 noon  _(error: 4 h counted from 8 am)_

**Working**

1. Head start = 2 × 40 = 80 km
2. Gain rate = 20 km/h ⇒ 4 h
3. 10 am + 4 h = 2 pm

**Formula:** Head start ÷ relative speed  
**Trap:** Count from B's start.

**Reviewer:** ☐ key ☐ stem ☐ distractors ☐ level ☐ fact — notes:

## QAA-260 · L3 · hard · Relative speed and meeting problems · officer

Two people 450 m apart walk towards each other at 4 km/h and 5 km/h. A dog runs to and fro between them at 12 km/h until they meet. The total distance the dog runs is:

- **A.** 450 m  _(error: initial gap reported)_
- **B.** 1,200 m  _(error: average walking speed used)_
- **C.** 1,080 m  _(error: time taken from one walker alone)_
- **D.** 600 m ✅

**Working**

1. Meeting time = 0.45/9 h = 0.05 h
2. Dog: 12 × 0.05 = 0.6 km = 600 m

**Formula:** Distance = speed × meeting time  
**Trap:** Only the time matters.

## QAA-261 · L3 · hard · Relative speed and meeting problems · officer

Two buses start at 9:00 am from towns 240 km apart and drive towards each other at 45 km/h and 30 km/h. The faster bus halts for 20 minutes on the way. They meet at:

- **A.** 12:24 pm ✅
- **B.** 12:20 pm  _(error: halt charged to the slower bus)_
- **C.** 12:32 pm  _(error: full halt added to meeting time)_
- **D.** 12:12 pm  _(error: halt ignored)_

**Working**

1. 45(t − 1/3) + 30t = 240
2. 75t = 255 ⇒ t = 3.4 h
3. 9:00 + 3 h 24 min = 12:24 pm

**Formula:** Moving time differs by the halt  
**Trap:** During the halt only one bus closes the gap.

**Reviewer:** ☐ key ☐ stem ☐ distractors ☐ level ☐ fact — notes:

## QAA-262 · L3 · hard · Relative speed and meeting problems · officer

**Working**

1. Gap after 2 h = 40 km
2. Closing speed = 100 km/h ⇒ 0.4 h
3. Total = 2 h 24 min

**Formula:** After turning: add speeds

## QAA-263 · L3 · hard · Relative speed and meeting problems · officer

A leaves Delhi at 6:00 am for Agra, 200 km away, at 50 km/h. B leaves Agra at 7:00 am for Delhi at 70 km/h. They meet at a distance from Delhi of:

- **C.** 62.5 km  _(error: A's first-hour lead left out)_

**Working**

1. By 7 am A has done 50 km; gap 150 km
2. Meet after 150/120 = 1.25 h
3. A: 50 + 62.5 = 112.5 km

## QAA-264 · L3 · hard · Relative speed and meeting problems · officer

Two people start from P and Q towards each other. They first meet 60 km from P. After reaching the far ends they turn back and meet again 40 km from Q. The distance PQ is:

- **C.** 220 km  _(error: 3 × 60 + 40)_

1. By the second meeting they cover 3 × PQ together
2. The P-starter covers 3 × 60 = 180 = PQ + 40
3. PQ = 140 km

**Formula:** Second meeting ⇒ 3× combined distance  
**Trap:** Each covers three times his first stretch.

## QAA-268 · L2 · medium · Races and circular tracks · foundation

## QAA-270 · L2 · medium · Races and circular tracks · foundation

**Working**

1. B takes 25 s ⇒ 4 m/s
2. In 20 s B covers 80 m
3. Margin = 20 m

## QAA-271 · L3 · hard · Races and circular tracks · officer

In a 1,000 m race, A beats B by 50 m and B beats C by 100 m. By how much does A beat C?

- **A.** 150 m  _(error: margins added)_

- **C.** 145 m ✅
- **D.** 140 m  _(error: B's margin scaled by 0.9)_

**Working**

1. When B runs 1,000, C runs 900
2. When B runs 950, C runs 900 × 0.95 = 855

## QAA-274 · L3 · hard · Races and circular tracks · officer

A and B run in opposite directions on a 1.5 km circular track at 18 km/h and 12 km/h, starting together. Not counting the start but counting the meeting at the starting point, how many times will they have met by the time they are first together again at the starting point?

## QAA-276 · L3 · hard · Races and circular tracks · officer

## QAA-283 · L2 · medium · Trains — crossing platforms and each other · foundation

- **A.** 66 km/h ✅

- **D.** 72 km/h  _(error: man's speed added twice)_

**Working**

1. Relative speed = 200/12 m/s = 60 km/h
2. Train = 60 + 6 = 66 km/h

**Formula:** Same direction: relative = train − man  
**Trap:** Add back the man's speed.

**Reviewer:** ☐ key ☐ stem ☐ distractors ☐ level ☐ fact — notes:

## QAA-284 · L2 · medium · Trains — crossing platforms and each other · foundation

Trains of 150 m and 200 m run on parallel tracks in opposite directions at 50 km/h and 76 km/h. They cross each other in:

- **A.** 4.29 s  _(error: only the shorter train's length)_
- **B.** 5.71 s  _(error: only the longer train's length)_
- **C.** 10 s ✅
- **D.** 48.46 s  _(error: same direction assumed)_

**Working**

1. Relative speed = 126 km/h = 35 m/s
2. Time = 350/35 = 10 s

**Formula:** Opposite: add speeds; add lengths  
**Trap:** Both lengths must pass.

**Reviewer:** ☐ key ☐ stem ☐ distractors ☐ level ☐ fact — notes:

## QAA-285 · L2 · medium · Trains — crossing platforms and each other · foundation

A train passes a pole in 15 s and a 100 m platform in 20 s. Its length is:

- **A.** 100 m  _(error: platform length)_
- **B.** 400 m  _(error: train + platform)_
- **C.** 300 m ✅
- **D.** 75 m  _(error: 100 × 15/20)_

**Working**

1. Extra 5 s covers 100 m ⇒ speed 20 m/s
2. Length = 20 × 15 = 300 m

**Formula:** Speed from the difference  
**Trap:** The extra time is for the platform.

**Reviewer:** ☐ key ☐ stem ☐ distractors ☐ level ☐ fact — notes:

## QAA-286 · L3 · hard · Trains — crossing platforms and each other · officer

A train overtakes two walkers going in its direction at 2 km/h and 4 km/h in 9 s and 10 s respectively. The train's length is:

- **A.** 61.11 m  _(error: train speed × 10 s)_
- **B.** 50 m ✅
- **C.** 55 m  _(error: walker's speed not subtracted)_
- **D.** 20 m  _(error: relative speed read as length)_

**Working**

1. (v − 2) × 9 = (v − 4) × 10 ⇒ v = 22 km/h
2. L = 20 × 5/18 × 9 = 50 m

**Formula:** Same length both times  
**Trap:** Use relative speeds.

**Reviewer:** ☐ key ☐ stem ☐ distractors ☐ level ☐ fact — notes:

## QAA-287 · L3 · hard · Trains — crossing platforms and each other · officer

Two trains of equal length run on parallel tracks in the same direction at 46 km/h and 36 km/h. The faster passes the slower in 36 s. The length of each train is:

- **A.** 410 m  _(error: opposite directions assumed)_
- **B.** 100 m  _(error: combined length)_
- **C.** 50 m ✅
- **D.** 25 m  _(error: halved twice)_

**Working**

1. Relative = 10 km/h = 25/9 m/s
2. Distance = 25/9 × 36 = 100 m = 2L
3. L = 50 m

**Formula:** Same direction: subtract  
**Trap:** Distance is both lengths.

## QAA-288 · L3 · hard · Trains — crossing platforms and each other · officer

A 300 m train overtakes a man running at 9 km/h in the same direction in 30 s. How long will it take to cross a 450 m platform?

- **A.** 60 s ✅
- **B.** 36 s  _(error: train length omitted)_
- **C.** 100 s  _(error: man's speed subtracted instead)_
- **D.** 75 s  _(error: man's speed not added back)_

**Working**

1. Relative = 10 m/s; man = 2.5 m/s ⇒ train 12.5 m/s
2. Time = 750/12.5 = 60 s

**Formula:** Recover true speed  
**Trap:** Relative speed was reduced by the man.

## QAA-289 · L3 · hard · Trains — crossing platforms and each other · officer

A train crosses a 150 m platform in 15 s and a 250 m platform in 20 s. Its speed is:

- **A.** 18 km/h  _(error: 100 m divided by 20 s)_
- **B.** 20 km/h  _(error: m/s written as km/h)_
- **C.** 41.14 km/h  _(error: platforms and times added)_
- **D.** 72 km/h ✅

**Working**

1. Extra 100 m in 5 s ⇒ 20 m/s
2. = 72 km/h

**Formula:** Speed = Δplatform ÷ Δtime  
**Trap:** Convert m/s to km/h.

## QAA-290 · L3 · hard · Trains — crossing platforms and each other · officer

A 250 m train running at 45 km/h crosses a bridge in 44 s. The bridge is:

- **A.** 800 m long  _(error: train length added)_
- **B.** 300 m long ✅

- **D.** 1,980 m long  _(error: km/h not converted)_

**Working**

1. 45 km/h = 12.5 m/s
2. Distance = 550 m = 250 + bridge ⇒ bridge 300 m

**Formula:** Distance = train + bridge  
**Trap:** Subtract the train.

## QAA-291 · L3 · hard · Trains — crossing platforms and each other · officer

A 180 m train at 72 km/h crosses a train coming from the opposite direction at 54 km/h in 12 s. The other train's length is:

- **B.** 240 m ✅
- **C.** 420 m  _(error: combined length reported)_
- **D.** 60 m  _(error: only first train's speed used)_

1. Relative = 126 km/h = 35 m/s
2. Combined = 420 m ⇒ other = 240 m

**Formula:** Opposite: add speeds  
**Trap:** Subtract the known length.

## QAA-292 · L3 · hard · Trains — crossing platforms and each other · officer

Train A (200 m, 90 km/h) overtakes train B (300 m) running in the same direction in 50 s. The speed of B is:

- **B.** 36 km/h  _(error: relative speed reported)_

- **D.** 126 km/h  _(error: opposite directions assumed)_

**Working**

1. Relative = 500/50 = 10 m/s = 36 km/h
2. B = 90 − 36 = 54 km/h

## QAA-293 · L3 · hard · Trains — crossing platforms and each other · officer

A train running at 54 km/h passes a platform in 36 s and a man standing on it in 20 s. The platform's length is:

- **A.** 300 m  _(error: train length reported)_
- **B.** 540 m  _(error: train + platform reported)_
- **C.** 240 m ✅
- **D.** 840 m  _(error: train length added)_

**Working**

1. 54 km/h = 15 m/s
2. Train = 300 m; train + platform = 540 m
3. Platform = 240 m

**Formula:** Subtract the two distances

## QAA-294 · L1 · easy · Boats and streams · foundation

A boat's downstream speed is 18 km/h and upstream speed is 12 km/h. The speed of the stream is:

- **A.** 3 km/h ✅
- **B.** 15 km/h  _(error: boat's speed)_
- **C.** 6 km/h  _(error: difference not halved)_
- **D.** 1.5 km/h  _(error: halved twice)_

**Working**

1. Stream = (18 − 12)/2 = 3

**Formula:** Stream = (D − U)/2  
**Trap:** Halve the difference.

**Reviewer:** ☐ key ☐ stem ☐ distractors ☐ level ☐ fact — notes:

## QAA-295 · L1 · easy · Boats and streams · foundation

A boat moves at 10 km/h in still water; the stream flows at 2 km/h. The time to go 36 km downstream is:

- **A.** 18 h  _(error: stream speed used)_
- **B.** 3 h ✅
- **C.** 4.5 h  _(error: upstream speed used)_
- **D.** 3.6 h  _(error: still-water speed used)_

**Working**

1. Downstream = 12 km/h
2. 36/12 = 3 h

**Formula:** Downstream = b + s  
**Trap:** Add the stream.

**Reviewer:** ☐ key ☐ stem ☐ distractors ☐ level ☐ fact — notes:


---

## QAA-296 · L2 · medium · Boats and streams · foundation

A boat goes 24 km upstream in 6 hours and 20 km downstream in 2 hours. Its speed in still water is:

- **A.** 5.5 km/h  _(error: total distance ÷ total time)_
- **B.** 3 km/h  _(error: stream speed)_
- **C.** 7 km/h ✅
- **D.** 14 km/h  _(error: not halved)_

**Working**

1. Up = 4, down = 10
2. Boat = (4 + 10)/2 = 7

**Formula:** Boat = (D + U)/2  
**Trap:** Averaging distance/time mixes legs.

**Reviewer:** ☐ key ☐ stem ☐ distractors ☐ level ☐ fact — notes:


---

## QAA-297 · L2 · medium · Boats and streams · foundation

A boat moves at 9 km/h in still water; the stream flows at 3 km/h. The time to go 24 km downstream and return is:

- **A.** 6 h ✅
- **B.** 4 h  _(error: upstream leg only)_
- **C.** 5 1/3 h  _(error: still-water speed for both legs)_
- **D.** 2 h  _(error: downstream leg only)_

**Working**

1. Down: 24/12 = 2 h; up: 24/6 = 4 h
2. Total 6 h

**Formula:** Time = d/(b + s) + d/(b − s)  
**Trap:** Legs take unequal times.

**Reviewer:** ☐ key ☐ stem ☐ distractors ☐ level ☐ fact — notes:


---

## QAA-298 · L2 · medium · Boats and streams · foundation

A man rows at 6 km/h in still water. Rowing upstream takes him twice as long as rowing the same distance downstream. The stream's speed is:

- **A.** 3 km/h  _(error: half the still-water speed)_
- **B.** 1.5 km/h  _(error: quarter of rowing speed)_
- **C.** 2 km/h ✅
- **D.** 4 km/h  _(error: downstream-upstream gap)_

**Working**

1. 6 + s = 2(6 − s)
2. s = 2

**Formula:** Time ratio ⇒ speed ratio (inverse)  
**Trap:** Downstream speed is twice upstream speed.

**Reviewer:** ☐ key ☐ stem ☐ distractors ☐ level ☐ fact — notes:


---

## QAA-299 · L3 · hard · Boats and streams · officer

A man rows 48 km downstream in 8 hours and 32 km upstream in 8 hours. How long will he take to row 30 km downstream and back?

- **A.** 12 h  _(error: still-water speed for both ways)_
- **B.** 12.5 h ✅
- **C.** 10 h  _(error: downstream speed both ways)_
- **D.** 15 h  _(error: upstream speed both ways)_

**Working**

1. Down 6, up 4 km/h
2. 30/6 + 30/4 = 5 + 7.5 = 12.5 h

**Formula:** Separate legs  
**Trap:** Never use the still-water speed for a trip in a stream.

**Reviewer:** ☐ key ☐ stem ☐ distractors ☐ level ☐ fact — notes:

## QAA-300 · L3 · hard · Boats and streams · officer

A boat with still-water speed 15 km/h goes 30 km downstream and returns in 4.5 hours in all. The speed of the stream is:

- **A.** 1.67 km/h  _(error: average speed subtracted from 15)_
- **B.** 5 km/h ✅
- **C.** 10 km/h  _(error: upstream speed)_
- **D.** 2.5 km/h  _(error: half the answer)_

**Working**

1. 30/(15 + s) + 30/(15 − s) = 4.5
2. 900/(225 − s²) = 4.5 ⇒ s² = 25 ⇒ s = 5

**Formula:** Round-trip equation  
**Trap:** Average speed is not boat − stream.

**Reviewer:** ☐ key ☐ stem ☐ distractors ☐ level ☐ fact — notes:


---

## QAA-301 · L3 · hard · Boats and streams · officer

A swimmer takes three times as long to swim a stretch upstream as downstream. The ratio of his still-water speed to the stream's speed is:

- **A.** 3:1  _(error: time ratio reported)_
- **B.** 1:2  _(error: ratio inverted)_
- **C.** 2:1 ✅
- **D.** 4:1  _(error: speed sum used)_

**Working**

1. (b + s)/(b − s) = 3
2. b = 2s ⇒ 2 : 1

**Formula:** Speed ratio = inverse time ratio  
**Trap:** Solve with componendo.

**Reviewer:** ☐ key ☐ stem ☐ distractors ☐ level ☐ fact — notes:


---

## QAA-302 · L3 · hard · Boats and streams · officer

A boat covers 20 km upstream and 44 km downstream in 8 hours; it covers 30 km upstream and 55 km downstream in 11 hours. Its speed in still water is:

- **A.** 3 km/h  _(error: stream speed)_
- **B.** 8 km/h ✅
- **C.** 5 km/h  _(error: upstream speed)_
- **D.** 16 km/h  _(error: not halved)_

**Working**

1. Let 1/U = a, 1/D = b: 20a + 44b = 8, 30a + 55b = 11
2. a = 1/5, b = 1/11 ⇒ U = 5, D = 11
3. Boat = 8

**Formula:** Solve for 1/U and 1/D  
**Trap:** Treat reciprocals as unknowns.

**Reviewer:** ☐ key ☐ stem ☐ distractors ☐ level ☐ fact — notes:


---

## QAA-303 · L3 · hard · Boats and streams · officer

A river flows at 2 km/h. A boat takes 50% more time upstream than downstream over the same distance. The boat's still-water speed is:

- **A.** 8 km/h  _(error: arithmetic slip in (b + 2)/(b − 2))_
- **B.** 12 km/h  _(error: downstream speed)_
- **C.** 10 km/h ✅
- **D.** 6 km/h  _(error: time ratio applied to stream)_

**Working**

1. (b + 2)/(b − 2) = 1.5
2. b + 2 = 1.5b − 3 ⇒ b = 10

**Formula:** Speed ratio = inverse time ratio  
**Trap:** 50% more time ⇒ ratio 3 : 2.

**Reviewer:** ☐ key ☐ stem ☐ distractors ☐ level ☐ fact — notes:


---

## QAA-304 · L3 · hard · Boats and streams · officer

A man rows 5 km downstream in the time he rows 3 km upstream. He rows 30 km downstream and back in 16 hours. The speed of the stream is:

- **A.** 3 km/h  _(error: upstream ratio term read as speed)_
- **B.** 4 km/h  _(error: still-water speed)_
- **C.** 2 km/h  _(error: difference not halved)_
- **D.** 1 km/h ✅

**Working**

1. D : U = 5 : 3 ⇒ D = 5k, U = 3k
2. 30/5k + 30/3k = 16 ⇒ 16/k = 16 ⇒ k = 1
3. Stream = (5 − 3)/2 = 1

**Formula:** Use the ratio then the total time  
**Trap:** Halve the difference.

**Reviewer:** ☐ key ☐ stem ☐ distractors ☐ level ☐ fact — notes:


---

## QAA-305 · L3 · hard · Boats and streams · officer

A boat's still-water speed is 14 km/h and the stream flows at 4 km/h. It takes 19 hours to go downstream from A to B and come back to C, midway between A and B. The distance AB is:

- **A.** 180 km ✅
- **B.** 90 km  _(error: AC reported)_
- **C.** 122.14 km  _(error: full distance used for the return)_
- **D.** 360 km  _(error: doubled)_

**Working**

1. Down 18, up 10 km/h
2. d/18 + (d/2)/10 = 19 ⇒ d(1/18 + 1/20) = 19
3. d = 180 km

**Formula:** Return is only half-way  
**Trap:** Upstream leg is d/2.

**Reviewer:** ☐ key ☐ stem ☐ distractors ☐ level ☐ fact — notes:


---

## QAA-306 · L3 · hard · Boats and streams · officer

A man rows at 7.5 km/h in still water. In a stream of 1.5 km/h he takes 50 minutes to row to a place and back. The distance to the place is:

- **A.** 6 km  _(error: round-trip distance)_
- **B.** 3 1/8 km  _(error: still-water speed for half the time)_
- **C.** 3 km ✅
- **D.** 2.5 km  _(error: rough split)_

**Working**

1. Down 9, up 6 km/h
2. d/9 + d/6 = 5/6 ⇒ 5d/18 = 5/6 ⇒ d = 3

**Formula:** Round-trip time equation  
**Trap:** Convert 50 min to 5/6 h.

**Reviewer:** ☐ key ☐ stem ☐ distractors ☐ level ☐ fact — notes:

## QAA-307 · L1 · easy · Individual and combined work rates · foundation

A can finish a job in 12 days and B in 24 days. Working together they finish it in:

- **A.** 18 days  _(error: average of the two times)_
- **B.** 6 days  _(error: half the faster time)_
- **C.** 8 days ✅
- **D.** 36 days  _(error: times added)_

**Working**

1. One day's work = 1/12 + 1/24 = 3/24 = 1/8
2. Together = 8 days

**Formula:** T = ab/(a + b)  
**Trap:** Add rates, not days.

**Reviewer:** ☐ key ☐ stem ☐ distractors ☐ level ☐ fact — notes:


---

## QAA-308 · L1 · easy · Individual and combined work rates · foundation

A, B and C can do a work in 10, 15 and 30 days respectively. Together they will finish it in:

- **A.** 5 days ✅
- **B.** 6 days  _(error: C left out)_
- **C.** 55 days  _(error: times added)_
- **D.** 18 1/3 days  _(error: average of the times)_

**Working**

1. 1/10 + 1/15 + 1/30 = 6/30 = 1/5
2. Together = 5 days

**Formula:** Sum of daily rates  
**Trap:** Every worker's rate counts.

**Reviewer:** ☐ key ☐ stem ☐ distractors ☐ level ☐ fact — notes:


---

## QAA-309 · L2 · medium · Individual and combined work rates · foundation

A and B together finish a task in 12 days. A alone takes 20 days. B alone will take:

- **A.** 16 days  _(error: average of 12 and 20)_
- **B.** 8 days  _(error: days subtracted)_
- **C.** 7.5 days  _(error: rates added instead of subtracted)_
- **D.** 30 days ✅

**Working**

1. B's rate = 1/12 − 1/20 = 2/60 = 1/30
2. B alone = 30 days

**Formula:** 1/B = 1/(A+B) − 1/A  
**Trap:** Subtract rates, not days.

**Reviewer:** ☐ key ☐ stem ☐ distractors ☐ level ☐ fact — notes:


---

## QAA-310 · L2 · medium · Individual and combined work rates · foundation

A is twice as efficient as B. Together they finish a job in 14 days. A alone would finish it in:

- **A.** 28 days  _(error: 14 × 2)_
- **B.** 42 days  _(error: B's time)_
- **C.** 7 days  _(error: 14 ÷ 2)_
- **D.** 21 days ✅

**Working**

1. Rates 2 : 1 ⇒ 3 units/day
2. Work = 3 × 14 = 42 units
3. A alone = 42/2 = 21 days

**Formula:** Work = combined rate × time  
**Trap:** Twice as efficient ⇒ half the time of B.

**Reviewer:** ☐ key ☐ stem ☐ distractors ☐ level ☐ fact — notes:


---

## QAA-311 · L2 · medium · Individual and combined work rates · foundation

6 workers finish a job in 15 days. How many workers are needed to finish it in 10 days?

- **A.** 11  _(error: one extra worker per day saved)_
- **B.** 10  _(error: days read as workers)_
- **C.** 9 ✅
- **D.** 4  _(error: direct proportion used)_

**Working**

1. Work = 6 × 15 = 90 worker-days
2. 90 ÷ 10 = 9 workers

**Formula:** M₁D₁ = M₂D₂  
**Trap:** Fewer days ⇒ more workers (inverse).

**Reviewer:** ☐ key ☐ stem ☐ distractors ☐ level ☐ fact — notes:


---

## QAA-312 · L3 · hard · Individual and combined work rates · officer

A and B can do a work in 15 days, B and C in 20 days, and C and A in 12 days. All three together will do it in:

- **A.** 15 2/3 days  _(error: average of the pair times)_
- **B.** 23.5 days  _(error: pair times added and halved)_
- **C.** 10 days ✅
- **D.** 5 days  _(error: sum of pairs not halved)_

**Working**

1. 2(A + B + C) = 1/15 + 1/20 + 1/12 = 12/60 = 1/5
2. A + B + C = 1/10 ⇒ 10 days

**Formula:** Add the pair rates and halve  
**Trap:** Each worker appears in two pairs.

**Reviewer:** ☐ key ☐ stem ☐ distractors ☐ level ☐ fact — notes:


---

## QAA-313 · L3 · hard · Individual and combined work rates · officer

A and B can do a work in 15 days, B and C in 20 days, and C and A in 12 days. A alone will do it in:

- **A.** 20 days ✅
- **B.** 10 days  _(error: all three together)_
- **C.** 60 days  _(error: B's time (C + A subtracted))_
- **D.** 30 days  _(error: C's time (A + B subtracted))_

**Working**

1. A + B + C = 1/10
2. A = 1/10 − (B + C) = 1/10 − 1/20 = 1/20
3. A alone = 20 days

**Formula:** Individual = total − opposite pair  
**Trap:** Subtract the pair that excludes A.

**Reviewer:** ☐ key ☐ stem ☐ distractors ☐ level ☐ fact — notes:


---

## QAA-314 · L3 · hard · Individual and combined work rates · officer

12 men or 18 women can finish a job in 14 days. In how many days will 8 men and 16 women finish it?

- **A.** 9 days ✅
- **B.** 17.5 days  _(error: heads of both groups added)_
- **C.** 7 days  _(error: women counted as men)_
- **D.** 10.5 days  _(error: men counted as women)_

**Working**

1. 12 men = 18 women ⇒ 1 man = 1.5 women; take man = 3, woman = 2 units
2. Work = 12 × 3 × 14 = 504
3. 8 × 3 + 16 × 2 = 56 per day ⇒ 9 days

**Formula:** Convert to a common unit  
**Trap:** Men and women have different efficiencies.

**Reviewer:** ☐ key ☐ stem ☐ distractors ☐ level ☐ fact — notes:


---

## QAA-315 · L3 · hard · Individual and combined work rates · officer

A can do a work in 20 days and B in 30 days. They start together, but A leaves 5 days before the work is finished. The work lasts:

- **A.** 17 days  _(error: 5 days added to the together time)_
- **B.** 10 days  _(error: A's working days reported)_
- **C.** 12 days  _(error: A's leaving ignored)_
- **D.** 15 days ✅

**Working**

1. B works T days, A works T − 5
2. (T − 5)/20 + T/30 = 1 ⇒ 5T = 75
3. T = 15 days

**Formula:** Σ(days × rate) = 1  
**Trap:** A misses the last 5 days, B does not.

**Reviewer:** ☐ key ☐ stem ☐ distractors ☐ level ☐ fact — notes:


---

## QAA-316 · L3 · hard · Individual and combined work rates · officer

A does 1/3 of a job in 5 days and B does 2/5 of it in 10 days. Working together, they finish the whole job in:

- **A.** 20 days  _(error: average of full times)_
- **B.** 40 days  _(error: full times added)_
- **C.** 9 3/8 days ✅
- **D.** 3 1/3 days  _(error: partial-job days used as full-job days)_

**Working**

1. A alone = 15 days; B alone = 25 days
2. Together = 15 × 25/40 = 75/8 = 9 3/8 days

**Formula:** Scale partial days to full job first  
**Trap:** 5 days is only a third of A's time.

**Reviewer:** ☐ key ☐ stem ☐ distractors ☐ level ☐ fact — notes:


---

## QAA-317 · L3 · hard · Individual and combined work rates · officer

4 men and 6 women finish a job in 8 days; 3 men and 7 women finish it in 10 days. In how many days will 10 women finish it?

- **A.** 3 7/11 days  _(error: 10 men instead of 10 women)_
- **B.** 5 1/11 days  _(error: man–woman ratio inverted)_
- **C.** 40 days ✅
- **D.** 8 days  _(error: men and women taken as equally efficient)_

**Working**

1. 8(4m + 6w) = 10(3m + 7w) ⇒ 2m = 22w ⇒ m = 11w
2. Work = 8(44 + 6) = 400 woman-days
3. 10 women ⇒ 40 days

**Formula:** Equate total work  
**Trap:** Solve the efficiency ratio first.

**Reviewer:** ☐ key ☐ stem ☐ distractors ☐ level ☐ fact — notes:


---

## QAA-318 · L3 · hard · Individual and combined work rates · officer

A is 50% more efficient than B, and B takes 30 days longer than A to finish a job alone. Working together they finish it in:

- **A.** 36 days ✅
- **B.** 75 days  _(error: average of individual times)_
- **C.** 18 days  _(error: together time halved)_
- **D.** 30 days  _(error: time difference reported)_

**Working**

1. Efficiency 3 : 2 ⇒ time 2 : 3 = 2k, 3k
2. k = 30 ⇒ A 60, B 90
3. Together = 60 × 90/150 = 36 days

**Formula:** Time ∝ 1/efficiency  
**Trap:** Convert efficiency to time first.

**Reviewer:** ☐ key ☐ stem ☐ distractors ☐ level ☐ fact — notes:


---

## QAA-319 · L2 · medium · Individual and combined work rates · officer

A, B and C can do a work in 10, 12 and 15 days. They start together; after 2 days C leaves and A and B finish the rest. The whole work takes:

- **A.** 5 5/11 days  _(error: C's work ignored)_
- **B.** 2 8/11 days  _(error: only the days after C left)_
- **C.** 4 8/11 days ✅
- **D.** 4 days  _(error: C assumed to stay)_

**Working**

1. 2 days' work = 2(6 + 5 + 4)/60 = 1/2
2. Rest 1/2 at 11/60 per day = 30/11 days
3. Total = 2 + 2 8/11 = 4 8/11 days

**Formula:** Work done + remaining work  
**Trap:** Add the first 2 days.

**Reviewer:** ☐ key ☐ stem ☐ distractors ☐ level ☐ fact — notes:


---

## QAA-320 · L3 · hard · Individual and combined work rates · officer

A can do a job in 6 days and B in 8 days. With C's help they finish it in 3 days and are paid ₹3,200 in all. C's share is:

- **A.** ₹1,066.67  _(error: equal three-way split)_
- **B.** ₹1,600  _(error: A's share)_
- **C.** ₹400 ✅
- **D.** ₹1,200  _(error: B's share)_

**Working**

1. In 3 days A does 1/2, B does 3/8
2. C does 1 − 1/2 − 3/8 = 1/8
3. C gets 3,200/8 = ₹400

**Formula:** Wages ∝ work done  
**Trap:** Share by work, not by days.

**Reviewer:** ☐ key ☐ stem ☐ distractors ☐ level ☐ fact — notes:


---

## QAA-321 · L3 · hard · Individual and combined work rates · officer

A can do a job in 24 days. A works alone for 6 days; then B joins and the two finish the rest in 9 more days. B alone would do the job in:

- **A.** 36 days  _(error: A's share and B's share swapped)_
- **B.** 24 days ✅
- **C.** 14.4 days  _(error: whole job taken as done together in 9 days)_
- **D.** 12 days  _(error: A's work in the last 9 days ignored)_

**Working**

1. A works 6 + 9 = 15 days ⇒ 15/24 = 5/8
2. B does 3/8 in 9 days
3. B alone = 24 days

**Formula:** Count each worker's total days  
**Trap:** A keeps working after B joins.

**Reviewer:** ☐ key ☐ stem ☐ distractors ☐ level ☐ fact — notes:

## QAA-322 · L1 · easy · Efficiency comparison and relative capacity · foundation

A is thrice as efficient as B. B alone finishes a job in 24 days. A alone will finish it in:

- **A.** 8 days ✅
- **B.** 72 days  _(error: multiplied instead of divided)_
- **C.** 12 days  _(error: twice as efficient assumed)_
- **D.** 6 days  _(error: together time)_

**Working**

1. Time ∝ 1/efficiency
2. A = 24/3 = 8 days

**Formula:** Time ∝ 1/efficiency  
**Trap:** More efficient ⇒ less time.

**Reviewer:** ☐ key ☐ stem ☐ distractors ☐ level ☐ fact — notes:

## QAA-323 · L1 · easy · Efficiency comparison and relative capacity · foundation

A is 25% more efficient than B. B alone does a job in 20 days. A alone will do it in:

- **A.** 16 days ✅
- **B.** 20 days  _(error: efficiency ignored)_
- **C.** 25 days  _(error: 25% added to the time)_
- **D.** 15 days  _(error: 25% taken off the time)_

**Working**

1. Efficiency 5 : 4 ⇒ time 4 : 5
2. A = 20 × 4/5 = 16 days

**Formula:** Time ∝ 1/efficiency  
**Trap:** 25% more efficiency is 20% less time.

**Reviewer:** ☐ key ☐ stem ☐ distractors ☐ level ☐ fact — notes:

## QAA-324 · L2 · medium · Efficiency comparison and relative capacity · foundation

The efficiencies of A and B are in the ratio 5 : 3. Together they finish a job in 15 days. B alone will take:

- **A.** 40 days ✅
- **B.** 24 days  _(error: A's time)_
- **C.** 25 days  _(error: 15 × 5/3)_
- **D.** 9 days  _(error: 15 × 3/5)_

**Working**

1. Work = (5 + 3) × 15 = 120 units
2. B alone = 120/3 = 40 days

**Formula:** Work = rate × time  
**Trap:** Use the combined rate.

**Reviewer:** ☐ key ☐ stem ☐ distractors ☐ level ☐ fact — notes:

## QAA-325 · L2 · medium · Efficiency comparison and relative capacity · foundation

A takes 10 days less than B to do a job, and A is twice as efficient as B. B alone will take:

- **A.** 6 2/3 days  _(error: together time)_
- **B.** 30 days  _(error: difference × 3)_
- **C.** 10 days  _(error: A's time)_
- **D.** 20 days ✅

**Working**

1. B = 2A and B − A = 10
2. A = 10, B = 20 days

**Formula:** Time ∝ 1/efficiency  
**Trap:** Twice as efficient ⇒ half the time.

**Reviewer:** ☐ key ☐ stem ☐ distractors ☐ level ☐ fact — notes:

## QAA-326 · L2 · medium · Efficiency comparison and relative capacity · foundation

3 men or 5 women can finish a job in 15 days. How long will 6 women take?

- **A.** 18 days  _(error: direct proportion)_
- **B.** 25 days  _(error: 5 × 15/3)_
- **C.** 7.5 days  _(error: women treated as men)_
- **D.** 12.5 days ✅

**Working**

1. Work = 5 × 15 = 75 woman-days
2. 6 women ⇒ 75/6 = 12.5 days

**Formula:** Convert to woman-days  
**Trap:** Use the women's rate.

**Reviewer:** ☐ key ☐ stem ☐ distractors ☐ level ☐ fact — notes:

## QAA-327 · L3 · hard · Efficiency comparison and relative capacity · officer

A is 40% more efficient than B. Together they finish a job in 35 days. A alone will finish it in:

- **A.** 49 days  _(error: 35 × 1.4)_
- **B.** 60 days ✅
- **C.** 84 days  _(error: B's time)_
- **D.** 70 days  _(error: together time doubled)_

**Working**

1. A : B = 7 : 5
2. Work = 12 × 35 = 420 units
3. A alone = 420/7 = 60 days

**Formula:** Efficiency ratio → units  
**Trap:** A's share is 7/12 of the rate.

**Reviewer:** ☐ key ☐ stem ☐ distractors ☐ level ☐ fact — notes:

## QAA-328 · L3 · hard · Efficiency comparison and relative capacity · officer

A finishes a job in 12 days. B is 60% more efficient than A. Working together they finish it in:

- **A.** 7.5 days  _(error: B's time)_
- **B.** 6 days  _(error: equal efficiency assumed)_
- **C.** 4.62 days ✅
- **D.** 5 days  _(error: 40% used instead of 60%)_

**Working**

1. Rates 1 : 1.6 ⇒ combined 2.6 A-units
2. Time = 12/2.6 = 60/13 = 4 8/13 days

**Formula:** Combined rate in A-units  
**Trap:** B's rate is 1.6 × A's.

**Reviewer:** ☐ key ☐ stem ☐ distractors ☐ level ☐ fact — notes:

## QAA-329 · L3 · hard · Efficiency comparison and relative capacity · officer

2 men do as much work as 3 women or 4 boys. 10 men finish a job in 18 days. In how many days will 4 men, 6 women and 8 boys finish it?

- **A.** 13.5 days  _(error: boys valued as women)_
- **B.** 10 days  _(error: all 18 persons taken as men)_
- **C.** 15 days ✅
- **D.** 12 6/7 days  _(error: women valued as men)_

**Working**

1. Man = 6, woman = 4, boy = 3 units
2. Work = 10 × 6 × 18 = 1,080
3. Group rate = 24 + 24 + 24 = 72 ⇒ 15 days

**Formula:** LCM of 2, 3, 4 gives unit rates  
**Trap:** Equal work ⇒ inverse efficiency ratio.

**Reviewer:** ☐ key ☐ stem ☐ distractors ☐ level ☐ fact — notes:

## QAA-330 · L3 · hard · Efficiency comparison and relative capacity · officer

A is twice as efficient as B, and together they finish a job in 12 days. If they work on alternate days starting with A, the job is finished in:

- **A.** 12 days  _(error: together time)_
- **B.** 24 days ✅
- **C.** 18 days  _(error: A's time alone)_
- **D.** 27 days  _(error: average of individual times)_

**Working**

1. A 18 days, B 36 days; work = 36 units, A 2/day, B 1/day
2. Every 2 days: 3 units ⇒ 12 cycles = 24 days

**Formula:** Cycle method  
**Trap:** Check whether the last cycle is partial.

**Reviewer:** ☐ key ☐ stem ☐ distractors ☐ level ☐ fact — notes:

## QAA-331 · L3 · hard · Efficiency comparison and relative capacity · officer

P is three times as efficient as Q and finishes a job 40 days earlier than Q. Together they finish it in:

- **A.** 20 days  _(error: P's time)_
- **B.** 40 days  _(error: difference reported)_
- **C.** 15 days ✅
- **D.** 60 days  _(error: Q's time)_

**Working**

1. Q = 3P and Q − P = 40 ⇒ P = 20, Q = 60
2. Together = 20 × 60/80 = 15 days

**Formula:** Time ∝ 1/efficiency  
**Trap:** Find the individual times first.

**Reviewer:** ☐ key ☐ stem ☐ distractors ☐ level ☐ fact — notes:

## QAA-332 · L3 · hard · Efficiency comparison and relative capacity · officer

5 men can do as much work as 8 women. 16 women finish a job in 25 days. In how many days will 10 men and 4 women finish it?

- **A.** 20 days ✅
- **B.** 25 days  _(error: 4 women omitted)_
- **C.** 28 4/7 days  _(error: men and women taken as equal)_
- **D.** 17 6/7 days  _(error: women counted as men)_

**Working**

1. Man = 8, woman = 5 units
2. Work = 16 × 5 × 25 = 2,000
3. Rate = 80 + 20 = 100 ⇒ 20 days

**Formula:** Common efficiency unit  
**Trap:** 5 men = 8 women ⇒ man : woman = 8 : 5.

**Reviewer:** ☐ key ☐ stem ☐ distractors ☐ level ☐ fact — notes:

## QAA-333 · L3 · hard · Efficiency comparison and relative capacity · officer

Machine M1 makes 300 units in 5 hours. Machine M2 is 20% less efficient than M1. Working together, how long will they take to make 1,080 units?

- **A.** 18 h  _(error: M1 alone)_
- **B.** 10 h ✅
- **C.** 8 2/11 h  _(error: M2 taken as 20% more efficient)_
- **D.** 22.5 h  _(error: M2 alone)_

**Working**

1. M1 = 60/h, M2 = 48/h
2. Together 108/h ⇒ 10 h

**Formula:** Units ÷ combined rate  
**Trap:** 20% less efficient ⇒ 0.8 × rate.

**Reviewer:** ☐ key ☐ stem ☐ distractors ☐ level ☐ fact — notes:

## QAA-334 · L3 · hard · Efficiency comparison and relative capacity · officer

A does 30% of a job in 6 days. B is 25% more efficient than A. Working together, in how many days will they finish the remaining 70%?

- **A.** 8 8/9 days  _(error: whole job instead of 70%)_
- **B.** 6 2/9 days ✅
- **C.** 11.2 days  _(error: B alone)_
- **D.** 6 days  _(error: 25% less time taken as 25% more efficiency)_

**Working**

1. A alone = 20 days; B = 1.25/20 = 1/16 per day
2. Together = 1/20 + 1/16 = 9/80
3. 0.7 ÷ 9/80 = 56/9 = 6 2/9 days

**Formula:** Remaining ÷ combined rate  
**Trap:** 25% more efficient ⇒ time × 4/5.

**Reviewer:** ☐ key ☐ stem ☐ distractors ☐ level ☐ fact — notes:

## QAA-335 · L2 · medium · Efficiency comparison and relative capacity · officer

The efficiencies of A, B and C are in the ratio 3 : 4 : 5. Together they finish a job in 20 days. C alone will take:

- **A.** 80 days  _(error: A's time)_
- **B.** 60 days  _(error: B's time)_
- **C.** 48 days ✅
- **D.** 33 1/3 days  _(error: 20 × 5/3)_

**Working**

1. Work = 12 × 20 = 240 units
2. C alone = 240/5 = 48 days

**Formula:** Units from efficiency ratio  
**Trap:** Multiply the combined rate by the time.

**Reviewer:** ☐ key ☐ stem ☐ distractors ☐ level ☐ fact — notes:

## QAA-336 · L3 · hard · Efficiency comparison and relative capacity · officer

If A works for 10 days and leaves, B finishes the rest in 15 days. If A works for 12 days and leaves, B finishes the rest in 12 days. B alone would finish the job in:

- **A.** 20 days  _(error: A's time)_
- **B.** 25 days  _(error: average of 20 and 30)_
- **C.** 12 days  _(error: together time)_
- **D.** 30 days ✅

**Working**

1. 12A + 12B = 1 ⇒ A + B = 1/12
2. 10A + 15B = 1 ⇒ 10/12 + 5B = 1 ⇒ B = 1/30
3. B alone = 30 days

**Formula:** Two equations in rates  
**Trap:** Second scenario gives the combined rate.

**Reviewer:** ☐ key ☐ stem ☐ distractors ☐ level ☐ fact — notes:

## QAA-337 · L1 · easy · Partial work and remaining-work problems · foundation

A can do a work in 20 days. A works for 8 days and then B finishes the rest in 9 days. B alone would do the work in:

- **A.** 27 days  _(error: 9 × 3)_
- **B.** 22.5 days  _(error: work done used instead of work left)_
- **C.** 12 days  _(error: 20 − 8)_
- **D.** 15 days ✅

**Working**

1. A does 8/20 = 2/5
2. B does 3/5 in 9 days ⇒ 15 days

**Formula:** Remaining work ÷ time  
**Trap:** B did 3/5, not the whole.

**Reviewer:** ☐ key ☐ stem ☐ distractors ☐ level ☐ fact — notes:

## QAA-338 · L1 · easy · Partial work and remaining-work problems · foundation

A can do a work in 15 days and B in 10 days. They work together for 4 days. What fraction of the work is left?

- **A.** 2/3  _(error: work done reported)_
- **B.** 1/6  _(error: one day's joint work)_
- **C.** 1/3 ✅
- **D.** 0.73  _(error: only A's work subtracted)_

**Working**

1. One day = 1/15 + 1/10 = 1/6
2. 4 days = 2/3 ⇒ left 1/3

**Formula:** Left = 1 − days × rate  
**Trap:** Include both workers.

**Reviewer:** ☐ key ☐ stem ☐ distractors ☐ level ☐ fact — notes:

## QAA-339 · L2 · medium · Partial work and remaining-work problems · foundation

A and B can do a work in 18 and 24 days. They work together for 6 days and then A leaves. B finishes the rest in:

- **A.** 7.5 days  _(error: A finishes instead of B)_
- **B.** 18 days  _(error: 24 − 6)_
- **C.** 10 days ✅
- **D.** 14 days  _(error: work done used)_

**Working**

1. 6 days = 6 × 7/72 = 7/12
2. Left 5/12 × 24 = 10 days

**Formula:** Remaining × individual time  
**Trap:** B finishes, so use 24.

**Reviewer:** ☐ key ☐ stem ☐ distractors ☐ level ☐ fact — notes:

## QAA-340 · L2 · medium · Partial work and remaining-work problems · foundation

A and B together can finish a job in 12 days. They work together for 5 days; B then leaves and A finishes the rest in 14 days. A alone would do the job in:

- **A.** 17 days  _(error: 12 + 5)_
- **B.** 33.6 days  _(error: work done used)_
- **C.** 19 days  _(error: 5 + 14)_
- **D.** 24 days ✅

**Working**

1. 5 days = 5/12
2. A does 7/12 in 14 days ⇒ 24 days

**Formula:** Remaining fraction ÷ time  
**Trap:** A did the remaining 7/12.

**Reviewer:** ☐ key ☐ stem ☐ distractors ☐ level ☐ fact — notes:

## QAA-341 · L2 · medium · Partial work and remaining-work problems · foundation

A can finish 3/5 of a job in 12 days. In how many more days will A finish the rest?

- **A.** 4.8 days  _(error: 12 × 2/5)_
- **B.** 8 days ✅
- **C.** 20 days  _(error: whole job time)_
- **D.** 18 days  _(error: 12 × 3/2)_

**Working**

1. 1/5 of the job = 4 days
2. Rest 2/5 = 8 days

**Formula:** Unitary method  
**Trap:** Scale by the remaining fraction.

**Reviewer:** ☐ key ☐ stem ☐ distractors ☐ level ☐ fact — notes:

## QAA-342 · L3 · hard · Partial work and remaining-work problems · officer

A, B and C can do a work in 20, 30 and 60 days. A works every day and is helped by B and C together on every third day. The work is finished in:

- **A.** 15 days ✅
- **B.** 14 days  _(error: help counted on alternate days)_
- **C.** 20 days  _(error: A alone)_
- **D.** 10 days  _(error: all three every day)_

**Working**

1. Units 60: A 3, B 2, C 1 per day
2. 3-day block = 3 + 3 + (3 + 2 + 1) = 12
3. 60/12 = 5 blocks = 15 days

**Formula:** Block method  
**Trap:** Help comes only on day 3, 6, 9…

**Reviewer:** ☐ key ☐ stem ☐ distractors ☐ level ☐ fact — notes:

## QAA-343 · L3 · hard · Partial work and remaining-work problems · officer

A can do a work in 12 days and B in 18 days. They work on alternate days, A starting. The work is finished in:

- **A.** 14 1/3 days ✅
- **B.** 7.2 days  _(error: together every day)_
- **C.** 15 days  _(error: last partial day rounded up)_
- **D.** 14.5 days  _(error: B assumed to start)_

**Working**

1. Units 36: A 3, B 2
2. 7 two-day cycles = 35 in 14 days
3. Day 15: A needs 1/3 day ⇒ 14 1/3 days

**Formula:** Cycle method, then partial day  
**Trap:** The last day need not be full.

**Reviewer:** ☐ key ☐ stem ☐ distractors ☐ level ☐ fact — notes:

## QAA-344 · L3 · hard · Partial work and remaining-work problems · officer

A and B can do a work in 10 and 15 days. They start together; A leaves after some days and B finishes the remaining work in 5 days. A left after:

- **A.** 6 days  _(error: together time)_
- **B.** 3 1/3 days  _(error: B's 5 days counted as A's)_
- **C.** 5 days  _(error: B's closing days copied)_
- **D.** 4 days ✅

**Working**

1. x/6 + 5/15 = 1
2. x/6 = 2/3 ⇒ x = 4

**Formula:** Joint phase + solo phase = 1  
**Trap:** B's last 5 days do 1/3.

**Reviewer:** ☐ key ☐ stem ☐ distractors ☐ level ☐ fact — notes:

## QAA-345 · L3 · hard · Partial work and remaining-work problems · officer

A and B together can do a work in 30 days. After working together for 18 days, A leaves and B finishes the rest in 20 days. A alone would do the work in:

- **A.** 38 days  _(error: 18 + 20)_
- **B.** 60 days  _(error: twice the together time)_
- **C.** 75 days ✅
- **D.** 50 days  _(error: B's time)_

**Working**

1. 18 days = 3/5 done
2. B does 2/5 in 20 ⇒ B = 50 days
3. A = 1/30 − 1/50 = 1/75 ⇒ 75 days

**Formula:** Find B, then subtract  
**Trap:** A's rate = joint − B's.

**Reviewer:** ☐ key ☐ stem ☐ distractors ☐ level ☐ fact — notes:

## QAA-346 · L3 · hard · Partial work and remaining-work problems · officer

20 workers can finish a project in 30 days. After 10 days, 5 workers leave. The remaining workers will finish the rest in:

- **A.** 26 2/3 days ✅
- **B.** 36 2/3 days  _(error: total duration reported)_
- **C.** 20 days  _(error: departure ignored)_
- **D.** 15 days  _(error: direct proportion 20 × 15/20)_

**Working**

1. Left = 20 × 20 = 400 worker-days
2. 15 workers ⇒ 400/15 = 26 2/3 days

**Formula:** Remaining worker-days  
**Trap:** Only 20 days' work was left.

**Reviewer:** ☐ key ☐ stem ☐ distractors ☐ level ☐ fact — notes:

## QAA-347 · L3 · hard · Partial work and remaining-work problems · officer

12 workers planned to finish a job in 16 days. After 4 days, 4 more workers join. How many days earlier than planned is the job finished?

- **A.** 3 days ✅
- **B.** 9 days  _(error: remaining days reported)_
- **C.** 4 days  _(error: joining workers read as days)_
- **D.** 12 days  _(error: planned remaining days)_

**Working**

1. Left = 12 × 12 = 144 worker-days
2. 16 workers ⇒ 9 days
3. Saved = 12 − 9 = 3 days

**Formula:** Remaining worker-days  
**Trap:** Compare with the 12 planned days.

**Reviewer:** ☐ key ☐ stem ☐ distractors ☐ level ☐ fact — notes:

## QAA-348 · L3 · hard · Partial work and remaining-work problems · officer

A can do 2/3 of a job in 16 days and B can do 3/4 of it in 12 days. B works alone for 4 days, then A and B finish the job together. The total time is:

- **A.** 9.6 days  _(error: together from the start)_
- **B.** 7.2 days  _(error: only the joint phase)_
- **C.** 11.2 days ✅
- **D.** 8 4/7 days  _(error: partial-job days used as full-job days)_

**Working**

1. A = 24, B = 16 days
2. B's 4 days = 1/4
3. 3/4 ÷ (1/24 + 1/16) = 3/4 × 48/5 = 7.2
4. Total = 11.2 days

**Formula:** Scale partial days first  
**Trap:** 2/3 in 16 days ⇒ 24 days for all.

**Reviewer:** ☐ key ☐ stem ☐ distractors ☐ level ☐ fact — notes:


---

## QAA-349 · L3 · hard · Partial work and remaining-work problems · officer

A can do a work in 40 days. A works for 5 days and B finishes the rest in 21 days. Working together from the start, A and B would finish it in:

- **A.** 15 days ✅
- **B.** 26 days  _(error: 5 + 21)_
- **C.** 24 days  _(error: B's time)_
- **D.** 32 days  _(error: average of individual times)_

**Working**

1. A does 1/8
2. B does 7/8 in 21 ⇒ B = 24 days
3. Together = 40 × 24/64 = 15 days

**Formula:** Find B from the remaining work  
**Trap:** Then combine rates.

**Reviewer:** ☐ key ☐ stem ☐ distractors ☐ level ☐ fact — notes:


---

## QAA-350 · L3 · hard · Partial work and remaining-work problems · officer

A and B can do a work in 12 days, and B and C in 16 days. A works for 5 days, then B for 7 days, and C finishes the rest in 13 days. C alone would do the work in:

- **A.** 13 days  _(error: C's closing days)_
- **B.** 16 days  _(error: B and C together)_
- **C.** 48 days  _(error: B's time)_
- **D.** 24 days ✅

**Working**

1. 5A + 7B + 13C = 5(A + B) + 2(B + C) + 11C
2. = 5/12 + 1/8 + 11C = 1 ⇒ C = 1/24
3. C alone = 24 days

**Formula:** Regroup into known pairs  
**Trap:** Split 7B as 5B + 2B.

**Reviewer:** ☐ key ☐ stem ☐ distractors ☐ level ☐ fact — notes:


---

## QAA-351 · L2 · medium · Partial work and remaining-work problems · officer

20 workers can finish a job in 25 days. After how many days should 5 more workers join so that the job finishes in 21 days?

- **A.** 4 days  _(error: 25 − 21)_
- **B.** 5 days ✅
- **C.** 10 days  _(error: half of 21 rounded)_
- **D.** 16 days  _(error: days the new workers work)_

**Working**

1. Work = 500 worker-days; 20 × 21 = 420
2. Extra 80 by 5 workers = 16 days
3. Join after 21 − 16 = 5 days

**Formula:** Total worker-days fixed  
**Trap:** The extra workers stay till the end.

**Reviewer:** ☐ key ☐ stem ☐ distractors ☐ level ☐ fact — notes:

## QAA-352 · L1 · easy · Pipes and cisterns · foundation

Pipes A and B can fill a tank in 12 hours and 15 hours. Both together fill it in:

- **A.** 27 h  _(error: times added)_
- **B.** 3 h  _(error: difference of the times)_
- **C.** 13.5 h  _(error: average of the times)_
- **D.** 6 2/3 h ✅

**Working**

1. 1/12 + 1/15 = 9/60 = 3/20
2. Time = 20/3 = 6 2/3 h

**Formula:** T = ab/(a + b)  
**Trap:** Add rates.

**Reviewer:** ☐ key ☐ stem ☐ distractors ☐ level ☐ fact — notes:

## QAA-353 · L1 · easy · Pipes and cisterns · foundation

Pipe A fills a tank in 10 hours; outlet B empties the full tank in 15 hours. With both open, the empty tank is filled in:

- **A.** 30 h ✅
- **B.** 5 h  _(error: difference of times)_
- **C.** 12.5 h  _(error: average of the times)_
- **D.** 6 h  _(error: outlet treated as inlet)_

**Working**

1. Net = 1/10 − 1/15 = 1/30
2. Time = 30 h

**Formula:** Net rate = fill − empty  
**Trap:** Subtract the outlet.

## QAA-354 · L2 · medium · Pipes and cisterns · foundation

Pipes A and B fill a tank in 20 and 30 hours; pipe C empties it in 60 hours. With all three open, the tank fills in:

- **A.** 10 h  _(error: C treated as an inlet)_
- **B.** 15 h ✅
- **C.** 30 h  _(error: B ignored)_
- **D.** 12 h  _(error: C ignored)_

**Working**

1. Net = 1/20 + 1/30 − 1/60 = 4/60
2. Time = 15 h

**Formula:** Net rate  
**Trap:** Only C is subtracted.

**Reviewer:** ☐ key ☐ stem ☐ distractors ☐ level ☐ fact — notes:

## QAA-355 · L2 · medium · Pipes and cisterns · foundation

A tap fills a tank in 8 hours, but because of a leak it takes 12 hours. The leak alone would empty the full tank in:

- **A.** 20 h  _(error: times added)_
- **B.** 4 h  _(error: difference of times)_
- **C.** 4.8 h  _(error: rates added)_
- **D.** 24 h ✅

**Working**

1. Leak rate = 1/8 − 1/12 = 1/24
2. Leak empties in 24 h

**Formula:** Leak = normal − actual rate  
**Trap:** Subtract rates, not times.

**Reviewer:** ☐ key ☐ stem ☐ distractors ☐ level ☐ fact — notes:

## QAA-356 · L2 · medium · Pipes and cisterns · foundation

Pipe A fills a tank in 6 hours and pipe B in 12 hours. Both are opened; after 2 hours B is closed. A fills the rest in:

- **A.** 6 h  _(error: B left running instead of A)_
- **B.** 3 h ✅
- **C.** 10 h  _(error: 12 − 2)_
- **D.** 4 h  _(error: 6 − 2)_

**Working**

1. 2 h together = 2 × 1/4 = 1/2
2. A fills 1/2 in 3 h

**Formula:** Remaining ÷ A's rate  
**Trap:** Half was already full.

**Reviewer:** ☐ key ☐ stem ☐ distractors ☐ level ☐ fact — notes:

## QAA-357 · L3 · hard · Pipes and cisterns · officer

Two pipes fill a cistern in 20 and 30 minutes; an outlet empties the full cistern in 15 minutes. With all three open on an empty cistern, it fills in:

- **A.** 6 2/3 min  _(error: outlet treated as inlet)_
- **B.** 12 min  _(error: outlet ignored)_
- **C.** 60 min ✅
- **D.** 15 min  _(error: outlet's time reported)_

**Working**

1. Net = 3/60 + 2/60 − 4/60 = 1/60
2. Time = 60 min

**Formula:** Net rate  
**Trap:** A small net rate means a long time.

**Reviewer:** ☐ key ☐ stem ☐ distractors ☐ level ☐ fact — notes:


---

## QAA-358 · L3 · hard · Pipes and cisterns · officer

Pipes A and B fill a tank in 16 h and 24 h. Both are opened together; B is closed after some time and the tank is full in 12 h from the start. B was open for:

- **A.** 4 h  _(error: 16 − 12)_
- **B.** 8 h  _(error: roles of A and B swapped)_
- **C.** 6 h ✅
- **D.** 9.6 h  _(error: together time)_

**Working**

1. A works 12 h ⇒ 3/4
2. B fills 1/4 ⇒ 24/4 = 6 h

**Formula:** Σ(time × rate) = 1  
**Trap:** A runs the full 12 h.

**Reviewer:** ☐ key ☐ stem ☐ distractors ☐ level ☐ fact — notes:


---

## QAA-359 · L3 · hard · Pipes and cisterns · officer

Pipes A, B and C can fill a tank in 12, 15 and 20 hours. All three are opened at 9:00 am on an empty tank; C is closed at 11:00 am. The tank is full at:

- **A.** 3:00 pm ✅
- **B.** 1:00 pm  _(error: remaining time counted from 9 am)_
- **C.** 6:12 pm  _(error: B's contribution ignored after 11 am)_
- **D.** 2:00 pm  _(error: C kept open throughout)_

**Working**

1. 2 h × 1/5 = 2/5 full
2. Rest 3/5 at 9/60 per h = 4 h
3. 11 am + 4 h = 3:00 pm

**Formula:** Phase-wise filling  
**Trap:** Count from 11 am.

**Reviewer:** ☐ key ☐ stem ☐ distractors ☐ level ☐ fact — notes:


---

## QAA-360 · L3 · hard · Pipes and cisterns · officer

Pipe A fills a tank in 4 hours; pipe B empties the full tank in 6 hours. Starting with an empty tank, they are opened alternately for 1 hour each, A first. The tank is full after:

- **A.** 18 h  _(error: last A-hour not counted)_
- **B.** 12 h  _(error: both open together)_
- **C.** 24 h  _(error: net 1 unit per 2 h for all 12 units)_
- **D.** 19 h ✅

**Working**

1. Units 12: A +3, B −2
2. Net +1 per 2 h; after 18 h level = 9
3. 19th hour A adds 3 ⇒ full at 19 h

**Formula:** Cycle method with a final check  
**Trap:** The tank fills during an A-hour before the cycle ends.

**Reviewer:** ☐ key ☐ stem ☐ distractors ☐ level ☐ fact — notes:


---

## QAA-361 · L3 · hard · Pipes and cisterns · officer

Pipes A and B fill a tank in 36 and 45 minutes. With outlet C also open, the tank fills in 30 minutes. C alone empties the full tank in:

- **A.** 81 min  _(error: 36 + 45)_
- **B.** 60 min ✅
- **C.** 12 min  _(error: C treated as an inlet)_
- **D.** 20 min  _(error: A and B together)_

**Working**

1. A + B = 1/36 + 1/45 = 1/20
2. C = 1/20 − 1/30 = 1/60
3. C empties in 60 min

**Formula:** Outlet = inlets − net  
**Trap:** Net filling is slower, so C drains.

**Reviewer:** ☐ key ☐ stem ☐ distractors ☐ level ☐ fact — notes:


---

## QAA-362 · L3 · hard · Pipes and cisterns · officer

Pipe A fills water three times as fast as pipe B. Together they fill a tank in 36 minutes. B alone will fill it in:

- **A.** 108 min  _(error: 36 × 3)_
- **B.** 144 min ✅
- **C.** 72 min  _(error: 36 × 2)_
- **D.** 48 min  _(error: A's time)_

**Working**

1. Rates 3 : 1 ⇒ 4 units/min; work = 144
2. B alone = 144 min

**Formula:** Units from rate ratio  
**Trap:** B has 1/4 of the combined rate.

**Reviewer:** ☐ key ☐ stem ☐ distractors ☐ level ☐ fact — notes:

## QAA-363 · L3 · hard · Pipes and cisterns · officer

An inlet fills a tank in 20 minutes. A leak drains water at 15 litres per minute, so with the leak the tank takes 30 minutes to fill. The capacity of the tank is:

- **A.** 450 litres  _(error: leak × filling time)_
- **B.** 900 litres ✅
- **C.** 1,800 litres  _(error: doubled)_
- **D.** 600 litres  _(error: leak × 40 (20 + 20))_

**Working**

1. Net rate = C/20 − 15
2. (C/20 − 15) × 30 = C ⇒ 1.5C − 450 = C
3. C = 900 L

**Formula:** Net rate × time = capacity  
**Trap:** Leak is in litres, rate in tanks.

**Reviewer:** ☐ key ☐ stem ☐ distractors ☐ level ☐ fact — notes:


---

## QAA-364 · L3 · hard · Pipes and cisterns · officer

Pipes A and B can fill a tank in 20 and 30 minutes. Only A is opened; after 5 minutes B is also opened. The tank is full after a total of:

- **A.** 14 min ✅
- **B.** 17 min  _(error: full joint time added to 5)_
- **C.** 12 min  _(error: both open from the start)_
- **D.** 9 min  _(error: time after B opened)_

**Working**

1. A alone 5 min = 1/4
2. 3/4 at 1/12 per min = 9 min
3. Total 14 min

**Formula:** Phase-wise filling  
**Trap:** Add the first 5 minutes.

**Reviewer:** ☐ key ☐ stem ☐ distractors ☐ level ☐ fact — notes:

## QAA-365 · L3 · hard · Pipes and cisterns · officer

A tank has inlets P and Q and an outlet R. P fills it in 10 h and R empties it in 15 h. When the tank is 1/3 full, all three are opened and the tank fills in 4 more hours. Q alone would fill it in:

- **A.** 7.5 h ✅
- **B.** 15 h  _(error: outlet ignored)_
- **C.** 6 h  _(error: Q's rate taken as the net rate 1/6)_
- **D.** 4.62 h  _(error: tank taken as empty at start)_

**Working**

1. Net rate = (2/3)/4 = 1/6
2. Q = 1/6 − 1/10 + 1/15 = 2/15
3. Q alone = 7.5 h

**Formula:** Net = P + Q − R  
**Trap:** Only 2/3 remained.

**Reviewer:** ☐ key ☐ stem ☐ distractors ☐ level ☐ fact — notes:
