## QAA-366 · L2 · medium · Pipes and cisterns · officer

Two pipes together fill a tank in 6 hours. One pipe alone would take 5 hours less than the other. The slower pipe alone fills it in:

- **A.** 15 h ✅
- **B.** 10 h  _(error: faster pipe's time)_
- **C.** 12 h  _(error: together time doubled)_
- **D.** 11 h  _(error: 6 + 5)_

**Working**

1. 1/x + 1/(x + 5) = 1/6
2. x = 10 ⇒ slower = 15 h

**Formula:** Quadratic from combined rate  
**Trap:** Answer the slower pipe.

**Reviewer:** ☐ key ☐ stem ☐ distractors ☐ level ☐ fact — notes:

## QAA-367 · L4 · hard · Percentage comparison — what percent of / more than · officer

An appliance shop's sales for one month are shown below. Every unit of an item was sold at the same discount on its marked price.

| Item | Units sold | Cost price per unit | Marked price per unit | Discount |
|---|---|---|---|---|
| Kettle | 120 | ₹800 | ₹1,200 | 20% |
| Toaster | 80 | ₹1,500 | ₹2,000 | 10% |
| Mixer | 60 | ₹2,400 | ₹3,200 | 15% |
| Iron | 150 | ₹500 | ₹750 | 12% |

Revenue from toasters is what percent more than revenue from kettles?

- **A.** 20%  _(error: toaster revenue used as base)_
- **B.** 11.11%  _(error: marked-price values compared)_
- **C.** 25% ✅
- **D.** 125%  _(error: ratio reported as 'more than')_

**Working**

1. Kettle SP = 960 ⇒ revenue 1,15,200
2. Toaster SP = 1,800 ⇒ revenue 1,44,000
3. (1,44,000 − 1,15,200)/1,15,200 = 25%

**Formula:** % more = difference ÷ base × 100  
**Trap:** Base is the kettle revenue.

**Reviewer:** ☐ key ☐ stem ☐ distractors ☐ level ☐ fact — notes:


---

## QAA-368 · L4 · hard · Combined transactions and overall gain · officer

An appliance shop's sales for one month are shown below. Every unit of an item was sold at the same discount on its marked price.

| Item | Units sold | Cost price per unit | Marked price per unit | Discount |
|---|---|---|---|---|
| Kettle | 120 | ₹800 | ₹1,200 | 20% |
| Toaster | 80 | ₹1,500 | ₹2,000 | 10% |
| Mixer | 60 | ₹2,400 | ₹3,200 | 15% |
| Iron | 150 | ₹500 | ₹750 | 12% |

The shop's overall profit percentage on the month's sales of all four items is closest to:

- **A.** 21.33%  _(error: simple average of item-wise profit %)_
- **B.** 19.86% ✅
- **C.** 16.57%  _(error: profit taken on selling price)_
- **D.** 39.89%  _(error: overall mark-up reported)_

**Working**

1. Total CP = 4,35,000; total SP = 5,21,400
2. Profit = 86,400
3. 86,400/4,35,000 = 19.86%

**Formula:** Overall % = total profit ÷ total cost  
**Trap:** Item percentages must be weighted by cost.

**Reviewer:** ☐ key ☐ stem ☐ distractors ☐ level ☐ fact — notes:

## QAA-369 · L4 · hard · Cost price, selling price and margin · officer

An appliance shop's sales for one month are shown below. Every unit of an item was sold at the same discount on its marked price.

| Item | Units sold | Cost price per unit | Marked price per unit | Discount |
|---|---|---|---|---|
| Kettle | 120 | ₹800 | ₹1,200 | 20% |
| Toaster | 80 | ₹1,500 | ₹2,000 | 10% |
| Mixer | 60 | ₹2,400 | ₹3,200 | 15% |
| Iron | 150 | ₹500 | ₹750 | 12% |

The profit on each mixer, expressed as a percentage of its selling price, is:

- **A.** 15%  _(error: discount rate reported)_
- **B.** 11.76% ✅
- **C.** 33.33%  _(error: mark-up reported)_
- **D.** 13.33%  _(error: profit taken on cost)_

**Working**

1. SP = 3,200 × 0.85 = 2,720
2. Profit = 320
3. 320/2,720 = 11.76%

**Formula:** Margin on SP = profit ÷ SP  
**Trap:** Margin and mark-up use different bases.

**Reviewer:** ☐ key ☐ stem ☐ distractors ☐ level ☐ fact — notes:

## QAA-370 · L4 · hard · Discount and marked price · officer

An appliance shop's sales for one month are shown below. Every unit of an item was sold at the same discount on its marked price.

| Item | Units sold | Cost price per unit | Marked price per unit | Discount |
|---|---|---|---|---|
| Kettle | 120 | ₹800 | ₹1,200 | 20% |
| Toaster | 80 | ₹1,500 | ₹2,000 | 10% |
| Mixer | 60 | ₹2,400 | ₹3,200 | 15% |
| Iron | 150 | ₹500 | ₹750 | 12% |

Next month the shop wants a 20% profit on mixers without changing the marked price. The maximum discount it can allow is:

- **A.** 10% ✅
- **B.** 20%  _(error: profit rate reported)_
- **C.** 11.11%  _(error: discount amount taken on the new SP)_
- **D.** 13.33%  _(error: discount amount taken on cost)_

**Working**

1. Required SP = 2,400 × 1.2 = 2,880
2. Discount = 320 on MP 3,200
3. = 10%

**Formula:** Discount % on marked price  
**Trap:** Discount is always on MP.

**Reviewer:** ☐ key ☐ stem ☐ distractors ☐ level ☐ fact — notes:

## QAA-371 · L4 · hard · Relative speed and meeting problems · officer

Stations X and Y are 360 km apart on a double-track line. Train P (240 m long) leaves X at 6:00 am for Y at 60 km/h. Train Q (210 m long) leaves Y at 7:30 am for X at 75 km/h. Train R (300 m long) leaves X at 8:00 am for Y at 100 km/h on the track used by P, and overtakes P on a loop line without either train slowing. All speeds are constant and there are no halts.

At what time do the engines of P and Q pass each other?

- **A.** 8:40 am  _(error: both assumed to start at 6:00 am)_
- **B.** 10:10 am  _(error: P's head start ignored)_
- **C.** 8:00 am  _(error: meeting time counted from 6:00 am)_
- **D.** 9:30 am ✅

**Working**

1. By 7:30 am P has run 90 km; gap 270 km
2. Closing speed 135 km/h ⇒ 2 h
3. Meet at 9:30 am

**Formula:** Head start, then closing speed  
**Trap:** Count the 2 h from 7:30 am.

**Reviewer:** ☐ key ☐ stem ☐ distractors ☐ level ☐ fact — notes:


---

## QAA-372 · L4 · hard · Trains — crossing platforms and each other · officer

Stations X and Y are 360 km apart on a double-track line. Train P (240 m long) leaves X at 6:00 am for Y at 60 km/h. Train Q (210 m long) leaves Y at 7:30 am for X at 75 km/h. Train R (300 m long) leaves X at 8:00 am for Y at 100 km/h on the track used by P, and overtakes P on a loop line without either train slowing. All speeds are constant and there are no halts.

How long do P and Q take to cross each other completely?

- **A.** 108 s  _(error: same-direction relative speed used)_
- **B.** 6.4 s  _(error: only P's length used)_
- **C.** 12 s ✅
- **D.** 3.33 s  _(error: km/h not converted to m/s)_

**Working**

1. Relative speed = 135 km/h = 37.5 m/s
2. Distance = 240 + 210 = 450 m
3. Time = 12 s

**Formula:** Opposite: add speeds; add lengths  
**Trap:** Convert with 5/18.

**Reviewer:** ☐ key ☐ stem ☐ distractors ☐ level ☐ fact — notes:


---

## QAA-373 · L4 · hard · Relative speed and meeting problems · officer

Stations X and Y are 360 km apart on a double-track line. Train P (240 m long) leaves X at 6:00 am for Y at 60 km/h. Train Q (210 m long) leaves Y at 7:30 am for X at 75 km/h. Train R (300 m long) leaves X at 8:00 am for Y at 100 km/h on the track used by P, and overtakes P on a loop line without either train slowing. All speeds are constant and there are no halts.

At what time does R's engine draw level with P's engine?

- **A.** 9:00 am  _(error: catch-up time counted from 6:00 am)_
- **B.** 11:00 am ✅
- **C.** 8:45 am  _(error: speeds added)_
- **D.** 10:00 am  _(error: head start divided by P's speed)_

**Working**

1. At 8:00 am P is 120 km ahead
2. Gain = 40 km/h ⇒ 3 h
3. 11:00 am (P has not yet reached Y)

**Formula:** Head start ÷ relative speed  
**Trap:** Count from R's departure.

**Reviewer:** ☐ key ☐ stem ☐ distractors ☐ level ☐ fact — notes:


---

## QAA-374 · L4 · hard · Trains — crossing platforms and each other · officer

Stations X and Y are 360 km apart on a double-track line. Train P (240 m long) leaves X at 6:00 am for Y at 60 km/h. Train Q (210 m long) leaves Y at 7:30 am for X at 75 km/h. Train R (300 m long) leaves X at 8:00 am for Y at 100 km/h on the track used by P, and overtakes P on a loop line without either train slowing. All speeds are constant and there are no halts.

How long does R take to overtake P completely (from R's engine reaching P's rear to R's rear clearing P's engine)?

- **A.** 21.6 s  _(error: only P's length used)_
- **B.** 12.15 s  _(error: opposite-direction relative speed)_
- **C.** 27 s  _(error: only R's length used)_
- **D.** 48.6 s ✅

**Working**

1. Relative speed = 40 km/h = 100/9 m/s
2. Distance = 300 + 240 = 540 m
3. Time = 48.6 s

**Formula:** Same direction: subtract speeds; add lengths  
**Trap:** Both lengths must clear.

**Reviewer:** ☐ key ☐ stem ☐ distractors ☐ level ☐ fact — notes:

## QAA-375 · L4 · hard · Boats and streams · officer

A ferry has a still-water speed of 18 km/h. It runs from jetty A downstream to jetty B, 48 km away, and returns to A. On the return it halts for 20 minutes at jetty C, midway between B and A. On a normal day the river flows at 6 km/h.

On a normal day, the total time for the round trip, including the halt, is:

- **A.** 4 h 20 min  _(error: return timed at downstream speed)_
- **B.** 5 h 40 min  _(error: still-water speed used both ways)_
- **C.** 6 h  _(error: halt ignored)_
- **D.** 6 h 20 min ✅

**Working**

1. Down: 48/24 = 2 h; up: 48/12 = 4 h
2. Total = 6 h + 20 min

**Formula:** Time = d/(b + s) + d/(b − s) + halt  
**Trap:** The upstream leg takes twice as long.

**Reviewer:** ☐ key ☐ stem ☐ distractors ☐ level ☐ fact — notes:


---

## QAA-376 · L4 · hard · Average speed and journey segments · officer

A ferry has a still-water speed of 18 km/h. It runs from jetty A downstream to jetty B, 48 km away, and returns to A. On the return it halts for 20 minutes at jetty C, midway between B and A. On a normal day the river flows at 6 km/h.

On a normal day, the ferry's average speed for the whole round trip, including the halt, is closest to:

- **A.** 13.71 km/h  _(error: halt taken as 1 hour)_
- **B.** 18 km/h  _(error: mean of downstream and upstream speeds)_
- **C.** 16 km/h  _(error: halt excluded)_
- **D.** 15.16 km/h ✅

**Working**

1. Distance 96 km; time 6 1/3 h
2. Average = 96 ÷ 19/3 = 15.16 km/h

**Formula:** Total distance ÷ total time  
**Trap:** Include the halt.

**Reviewer:** ☐ key ☐ stem ☐ distractors ☐ level ☐ fact — notes:


---

## QAA-377 · L4 · hard · Boats and streams · officer

A ferry has a still-water speed of 18 km/h. It runs from jetty A downstream to jetty B, 48 km away, and returns to A. On the return it halts for 20 minutes at jetty C, midway between B and A. On a normal day the river flows at 6 km/h.

On a flood day the ferry makes the round trip, without the halt, in 9 hours 36 minutes. The stream speed that day is:

- **A.** 10 km/h  _(error: round-trip average speed reported)_
- **B.** 6 km/h  _(error: normal-day stream speed)_
- **C.** 12 km/h ✅
- **D.** 8 km/h  _(error: average speed subtracted from still-water speed)_

**Working**

1. 48/(18 + s) + 48/(18 − s) = 9.6
2. 1,728/(324 − s²) = 9.6 ⇒ s² = 144
3. s = 12 km/h

**Formula:** Round-trip equation  
**Trap:** Average speed ≠ boat − stream.

**Reviewer:** ☐ key ☐ stem ☐ distractors ☐ level ☐ fact — notes:


---

## QAA-378 · L4 · hard · Average speed and journey segments · officer

A ferry has a still-water speed of 18 km/h. It runs from jetty A downstream to jetty B, 48 km away, and returns to A. On the return it halts for 20 minutes at jetty C, midway between B and A. On a normal day the river flows at 6 km/h.

On a normal day the operator wants the round trip, including the halt, completed in 5 hours 40 minutes by speeding up only on the return leg. The still-water speed needed on the return is:

- **A.** 8.4 km/h  _(error: stream subtracted instead of added)_
- **B.** 14.4 km/h  _(error: required ground speed reported)_
- **C.** 19.09 km/h  _(error: halt ignored)_
- **D.** 20.4 km/h ✅

**Working**

1. Downstream 2 h + halt 1/3 h
2. Return must take 5 2/3 − 2 1/3 = 3 1/3 h ⇒ ground speed 14.4 km/h
3. Still-water speed = 14.4 + 6 = 20.4 km/h

**Formula:** Remaining distance ÷ remaining time, then add stream  
**Trap:** Upstream ground speed = b − s.

**Reviewer:** ☐ key ☐ stem ☐ distractors ☐ level ☐ fact — notes:


---

## QAA-379 · L4 · hard · Simple and compound ratio · officer

Asha, Bilal and Chitra run a catering business for one year. Asha invests ₹1,20,000 at the start. Bilal invests ₹90,000 at the start and withdraws ₹30,000 at the end of the 4th month. Chitra joins at the end of the 3rd month with ₹1,60,000. Asha, the working partner, first takes 10% of the annual profit as a management fee; the rest is shared in the ratio of capital-months. The annual profit is ₹3,10,000.

The ratio in which the profit after the fee is shared (Asha : Bilal : Chitra) is:

- **A.** 12:9:16  _(error: opening capitals only)_
- **B.** 4:3:4  _(error: Bilal's withdrawal ignored)_
- **C.** 12:7:16  _(error: Chitra counted for 12 months)_
- **D.** 12:7:12 ✅

**Working**

1. Asha 1,20,000 × 12 = 14,40,000
2. Bilal 90,000 × 4 + 60,000 × 8 = 8,40,000
3. Chitra 1,60,000 × 9 = 14,40,000 ⇒ 12 : 7 : 12

**Formula:** Compound ratio: capital × months  
**Trap:** Split Bilal's year at the withdrawal.

**Reviewer:** ☐ key ☐ stem ☐ distractors ☐ level ☐ fact — notes:


---

## QAA-380 · L4 · hard · Partnership and profit sharing · officer

Asha, Bilal and Chitra run a catering business for one year. Asha invests ₹1,20,000 at the start. Bilal invests ₹90,000 at the start and withdraws ₹30,000 at the end of the 4th month. Chitra joins at the end of the 3rd month with ₹1,60,000. Asha, the working partner, first takes 10% of the annual profit as a management fee; the rest is shared in the ratio of capital-months. The annual profit is ₹3,10,000.

Bilal's share of the profit is:

- **A.** ₹63,000 ✅
- **B.** ₹70,000  _(error: fee not deducted)_
- **C.** ₹76,090.91  _(error: withdrawal ignored (4 : 3 : 4))_
- **D.** ₹93,000  _(error: equal split)_

**Working**

1. Pool = 3,10,000 − 31,000 = 2,79,000
2. Bilal = 2,79,000 × 7/31 = ₹63,000

**Formula:** Share = pool × own ratio ÷ total  
**Trap:** Deduct the fee first.

**Reviewer:** ☐ key ☐ stem ☐ distractors ☐ level ☐ fact — notes:


---

## QAA-381 · L4 · hard · Partnership and profit sharing · officer

Asha, Bilal and Chitra run a catering business for one year. Asha invests ₹1,20,000 at the start. Bilal invests ₹90,000 at the start and withdraws ₹30,000 at the end of the 4th month. Chitra joins at the end of the 3rd month with ₹1,60,000. Asha, the working partner, first takes 10% of the annual profit as a management fee; the rest is shared in the ratio of capital-months. The annual profit is ₹3,10,000.

Asha's total receipt from the business for the year is:

- **A.** ₹1,39,000 ✅
- **B.** ₹1,08,000  _(error: fee omitted)_
- **C.** ₹1,51,000  _(error: fee added without deducting it from the pool)_
- **D.** ₹1,20,000  _(error: whole profit shared, no fee)_

**Working**

1. Asha's share = 2,79,000 × 12/31 = 1,08,000
2. Plus fee 31,000 = ₹1,39,000

**Formula:** Working partner: fee + share  
**Trap:** The fee comes out of the profit before sharing.

**Reviewer:** ☐ key ☐ stem ☐ distractors ☐ level ☐ fact — notes:


---

## QAA-382 · L4 · hard · Partnership and profit sharing · officer

Asha, Bilal and Chitra run a catering business for one year. Asha invests ₹1,20,000 at the start. Bilal invests ₹90,000 at the start and withdraws ₹30,000 at the end of the 4th month. Chitra joins at the end of the 3rd month with ₹1,60,000. Asha, the working partner, first takes 10% of the annual profit as a management fee; the rest is shared in the ratio of capital-months. The annual profit is ₹3,10,000.

By what percentage does Asha's total receipt exceed Chitra's?

- **A.** 0%  _(error: fee overlooked (equal capital-months))_
- **B.** 28.7% ✅
- **C.** 10%  _(error: fee rate reported)_
- **D.** 22.3%  _(error: Asha's receipt used as base)_

**Working**

1. Asha 1,39,000; Chitra 1,08,000
2. Excess 31,000/1,08,000 = 28.70%

**Formula:** % more = difference ÷ base  
**Trap:** Base is Chitra's receipt.

**Reviewer:** ☐ key ☐ stem ☐ distractors ☐ level ☐ fact — notes:

## QAA-383 · L4 · hard · Proportional division of quantities · officer

Asha, Bilal and Chitra run a catering business for one year. Asha invests ₹1,20,000 at the start. Bilal invests ₹90,000 at the start and withdraws ₹30,000 at the end of the 4th month. Chitra joins at the end of the 3rd month with ₹1,60,000. Asha, the working partner, first takes 10% of the annual profit as a management fee; the rest is shared in the ratio of capital-months. The annual profit is ₹3,10,000.

Chitra divides her share among her three children in the ratio 1/3 : 1/4 : 1/6. The largest portion is:

- **A.** ₹36,000  _(error: 1/3 of the total taken directly)_
- **B.** ₹24,000  _(error: smallest portion)_
- **C.** ₹12,000  _(error: one ratio part)_
- **D.** ₹48,000 ✅

**Working**

1. 1/3 : 1/4 : 1/6 = 4 : 3 : 2 (× 12)
2. 1,08,000 ÷ 9 = 12,000 per part
3. Largest = 4 × 12,000 = ₹48,000

**Formula:** Clear fractions with the LCM  
**Trap:** Fractional ratios must be converted to whole numbers.

**Reviewer:** ☐ key ☐ stem ☐ distractors ☐ level ☐ fact — notes:

## QAA-384 · L4 · hard · Mixtures and alligation · officer

A dairy has three tanks. Tank X holds 60 litres of milk and water in the ratio 5 : 1; tank Y holds 80 litres in the ratio 3 : 1; tank Z holds 40 litres of pure milk. Milk costs the dairy ₹48 per litre; water costs nothing.

If tanks X and Y are emptied into one container, the ratio of milk to water in it is:

- **A.** 11:3 ✅
- **B.** 4:1  _(error: ratio terms added)_
- **C.** 3:11  _(error: ratio inverted)_
- **D.** 19:5  _(error: unweighted mean of concentrations)_

**Working**

1. X: 50 milk, 10 water; Y: 60 milk, 20 water
2. Total 110 : 30 = 11 : 3

**Formula:** Add actual quantities  
**Trap:** Ratio terms cannot be added across different volumes.

**Reviewer:** ☐ key ☐ stem ☐ distractors ☐ level ☐ fact — notes:

## QAA-385 · L4 · hard · Mixtures and alligation · officer

A dairy has three tanks. Tank X holds 60 litres of milk and water in the ratio 5 : 1; tank Y holds 80 litres in the ratio 3 : 1; tank Z holds 40 litres of pure milk. Milk costs the dairy ₹48 per litre; water costs nothing.

How many litres from tank Z must be added to the whole of tank Y so that milk makes up 80% of the mixture?

- **A.** 4 litres  _(error: extra milk for 80 L only, volume increase ignored)_
- **B.** 16 litres  _(error: shortfall divided by water fraction)_
- **C.** 5 litres  _(error: alligation ratio 1 part read as litres)_
- **D.** 20 litres ✅

**Working**

1. Alligation: Y 75%, Z 100%, target 80% ⇒ Y : Z = 20 : 5 = 4 : 1
2. Z = 80/4 = 20 L
3. Check: 80/100 = 80%

**Formula:** Alligation  
**Trap:** Adding milk also raises the volume.

**Reviewer:** ☐ key ☐ stem ☐ distractors ☐ level ☐ fact — notes:

## QAA-386 · L4 · hard · Averages — simple and weighted · officer

A dairy has three tanks. Tank X holds 60 litres of milk and water in the ratio 5 : 1; tank Y holds 80 litres in the ratio 3 : 1; tank Z holds 40 litres of pure milk. Milk costs the dairy ₹48 per litre; water costs nothing.

If all three tanks are mixed, the average cost per litre of the mixture is:

- **A.** ₹48  _(error: price of pure milk)_
- **B.** ₹40 ✅
- **C.** ₹41.33  _(error: simple average of the tanks' costs per litre)_
- **D.** ₹36  _(error: tank Y's cost per litre)_

**Working**

1. Milk = 50 + 60 + 40 = 150 L in 180 L
2. Cost = 150 × 48 = 7,200
3. Average = 7,200/180 = ₹40 per litre

**Formula:** Weighted average = total cost ÷ total volume  
**Trap:** Tanks differ in volume.

**Reviewer:** ☐ key ☐ stem ☐ distractors ☐ level ☐ fact — notes:

## QAA-387 · L4 · hard · Mixtures and alligation · officer

A dairy has three tanks. Tank X holds 60 litres of milk and water in the ratio 5 : 1; tank Y holds 80 litres in the ratio 3 : 1; tank Z holds 40 litres of pure milk. Milk costs the dairy ₹48 per litre; water costs nothing.

From tank Y, 16 litres are drawn off and replaced with water; this is done twice. The milk left in tank Y is:

- **A.** 36 litres  _(error: same milk removed each time (linear))_
- **B.** 48 litres  _(error: only one operation)_
- **C.** 38.4 litres ✅
- **D.** 51.2 litres  _(error: factor applied to total liquid)_

**Working**

1. Each draw keeps 64/80 = 4/5 of the milk
2. 60 × (4/5)² = 38.4 L

**Formula:** Milk left = M(1 − r/V)ⁿ  
**Trap:** The second draw removes less milk.

**Reviewer:** ☐ key ☐ stem ☐ distractors ☐ level ☐ fact — notes:

## QAA-388 · L4 · hard · Median and mode · officer

Marks (out of 10) scored by 25 candidates in a mock test:

| Marks | 3 | 4 | 5 | 6 | 7 | 8 | 9 |
|---|---|---|---|---|---|---|---|
| Candidates | 2 | 3 | 7 | 4 | 4 | 3 | 2 |

The median mark is:

- **A.** 5  _(error: mode reported)_
- **B.** 5.5  _(error: even count assumed (12th and 13th averaged))_
- **C.** 6 ✅
- **D.** 5.88  _(error: mean reported)_

**Working**

1. Cumulative: 2, 5, 12, 16, …
2. Median = 13th value = 6

**Formula:** Median = ((n + 1)/2)th value  
**Trap:** Use cumulative frequencies.

**Reviewer:** ☐ key ☐ stem ☐ distractors ☐ level ☐ fact — notes:

## QAA-389 · L4 · hard · Median and mode · officer

Marks (out of 10) scored by 25 candidates in a mock test:

| Marks | 3 | 4 | 5 | 6 | 7 | 8 | 9 |
|---|---|---|---|---|---|---|---|
| Candidates | 2 | 3 | 7 | 4 | 4 | 3 | 2 |

Three more candidates, each scoring 7, are added. The new median and mode are:

- **A.** Median 6; modes 5 and 7 ✅
- **B.** Median 6; mode 7 only  _(error: tie with 5 missed)_
- **C.** Median 7; modes 5 and 7  _(error: median shifted to the added value)_
- **D.** Median 6.5; mode 7  _(error: median taken between 6 and 7; old mode dropped)_

**Working**

1. n = 28 ⇒ median = mean of 14th and 15th values
2. Cumulative: 2, 5, 12, 16 ⇒ both are 6 ⇒ median 6
3. 5 and 7 now both occur 7 times ⇒ bimodal

**Formula:** Median for even n; mode = most frequent value(s)  
**Trap:** A tie gives two modes.

**Reviewer:** ☐ key ☐ stem ☐ distractors ☐ level ☐ fact — notes:


---

## QAA-390 · L4 · hard · Median and mode · officer

Marks (out of 10) scored by 25 candidates in a mock test:

| Marks | 3 | 4 | 5 | 6 | 7 | 8 | 9 |
|---|---|---|---|---|---|---|---|
| Candidates | 2 | 3 | 7 | 4 | 4 | 3 | 2 |

What is the least number of additional candidates, each scoring 8, needed to make the median 7 or more?

- **A.** 7  _(error: median of 6.5 accepted)_
- **B.** 9  _(error: even total assumed necessary)_
- **C.** 4  _(error: difference of cumulative counts (16 − 12))_
- **D.** 8 ✅

**Working**

1. With k added, n = 25 + k; 16 candidates score 6 or less
2. Need the middle position(s) beyond 16
3. k = 7 ⇒ n = 32, median = (6 + 7)/2 = 6.5; k = 8 ⇒ n = 33, median = 17th = 7

**Formula:** Median position (n + 1)/2  
**Trap:** An even total averages two middle values.

**Reviewer:** ☐ key ☐ stem ☐ distractors ☐ level ☐ fact — notes:
